> Enoch’s white horse, and Enoch’s ocean-spoil
 

 In ocean-smelling osier—

===Chinese===
> 伊诺克的白马，还有伊诺克海上的斩获

盛在透着海腥的柳条筐里——

===Original===
(i.e. in a fish-basket)

===Chinese===
（也就是盛鱼的篓子）

===Original===
> —and his face
 

 Rough-reddened with a thousand winter gales,
 

 Not only to the market town were known,
 

 But in the leafy lanes beyond the down
 

 Far as the portal-warding lion-whelp
 

 And peacock yewtree of the lonely Hall
 

 Whose Friday fare was Enoch’s ministering,

===Chinese===
> ——还有他的那张脸，

叫千百个冬天的狂风刮得粗糙通红，

不但在那集市镇上尽人皆知，

便在丘陵外绿叶夹道的小巷深处，

远至那座孤宅门前守户的幼狮

与孔雀般的紫杉，也一样闻名——

那宅子星期五的饭食，全靠伊诺克操办，

===Original===
why, then we feel that the vehicle is altogether too pompous for its load, and those who make speech too pompous for its content commit, albeit in varying degrees, the error of Defoe’s religious lady who, seeing a bottle of overripe beer explode and cork and froth fly up to the ceiling, cried out, “O, the wonders of Omnipotent Power!” The poet who commends fresh fish to us as “ocean-spoil” can cast no stone at his brother who writes of them as “the finny denizens of the deep,” or even at his cousin the journalist, who exalts the oyster into a “succulent bivalve”—

===Chinese===
咳，那时我们便感到，这辆车对于它载的货实在太过排场；凡把言语弄得排场得超过内容者，都在犯着——尽管程度不一——笛福（Defoe）笔下那位虔敬太太的错误：她眼见一瓶过熟的啤酒炸了开来，软木塞和泡沫直飞上天花板，便喊道：「哦，全能者之权能，何等奇妙！」那位把鲜鱼向我们美称为「海上的斩获」（ocean-spoil）的诗人，没法向把鱼写成「深渊里有鳍的居民」（the finny denizens of the deep）的同行掷石头，甚至也没法向他的表亲、那位把牡蛎擢升为「多汁的双壳贝」（succulent bivalve）的新闻记者掷石头——

===Original===
> The feathered tribes on pinions cleave the air;
 

 Not so the mackerel, and, still less, the bear!

===Chinese===
> 有羽的族类振翮剖开大气；

鲭鱼便不这样，狗熊更不消说了！

===Original===
I believe this difficulty, which verse, by nature and origin emotional, encounters in dealing with ordinary unemotional narrative, to lie as a technical reason at the bottom of Horace’s advice to the writer of Epic to plunge *in medias res*, thus avoiding flat preparative and catching at once a high wind which shall carry him hereafter across dull levels and intervals. I believe that it lay—though whether consciously or not he scarcely tells us—at the bottom of Matthew Arnold’s mind when, selecting certain qualities for which to praise Homer, he chose, for the very first, Homer’s *rapidity*. “First,” he says, “Homer is eminently rapid; and,” he adds justly, “to this rapidity the elaborate movement of Miltonic blank verse is alien.”

===Chinese===
我相信，这一困难——本性与起源皆属情感的诗歌，在处理平常而不动情感的叙事时所遭遇的困难——正作为一条技术性的理由，垫在贺拉斯（Horace）那条忠告的底下：他忠告史诗作者直插*in medias res*（故事的中心），如此既避开平板的开场铺垫，又能即刻兜住一股劲风，好载着他此后渡过沉闷的平川与间隔。我也相信，它同样垫在马修·阿诺德（Matthew Arnold）的心底——尽管他几乎不曾告诉我们是有意还是无意——当他为赞美荷马而挑选若干品质时，他放在头一位的，正是荷马的*迅疾*（rapidity）。「首先，」他说，「荷马极其迅疾；」他又中肯地补上一句，「弥尔顿式素体诗那精雕细琢的行进，与这种迅疾是格格不入的。」

===Original===
Now until one studies writing as an art, trying to discover what this or that form of it accomplishes with ease and what with difficulty, and why verse can do one thing and prose another, Arnold’s choice of *rapidity* to put in the forefront of Homer’s merits may seem merely capricious. “Homer (we say) has other great qualities. Arnold himself indicates Homer’s simplicity, directness, nobility. Surely either one of these should be mentioned before rapidity, in itself not comparable as a virtue with either?”

===Chinese===
喏，除非一个人把写作当作一门艺术来研究，着力去发现它的这种或那种形式做什么是轻而易举、做什么难乎其难，又为什么诗能做这一件而散文能做那一件，否则，阿诺德把*迅疾*放到荷马诸美德最前列的选择，看上去便纯属任性。「荷马（我们会说）另有种种伟大的品质。阿诺德自己就点出了荷马的质朴、直截、高贵。这两者随便哪一个，总该排在迅疾之前吧——就其本身而论，迅疾作为一种美德，哪能与它们任何一个相提并论？」

===Original===
But when we see that the difficulty of verse-narrative lies just *here*; that the epic poet who is rapid has met, and has overcome, the capital difficulty of his form, then we begin to do justice not only to Arnold as a critic but (which is of far higher moment) to Homer as a craftsman.

===Chinese===
可是一旦我们看清：诗体叙事的困难恰恰*落在这里*；看到一位行文迅疾的史诗诗人，已经迎头遇上了他这一形式的大难点，并且战胜了它——那时我们才开始公正地对待不仅是作为批评家的阿诺德，而且（这更要紧得多）是作为匠人的荷马。

===Original===
The genius of Homer in this matter is in fact something daemonic. He seems to shirk nothing: and the effect of this upon critics is bewildering. The acutest of them are left wondering how on earth an ordinary tale—say of how some mariners beached ship, stowed sail, walked ashore and cooked their dinner—can be made so poetical. They are inclined to divide the credit between the poet and his fortunate age—“a time” suggests Pater “in which one could hardly have spoken at all without ideal effect, or the sailors pulled down their boat without making a picture ‘in the great style’ against a sky charged with marvels.”

===Chinese===
荷马在这件事上的天才，实际上带有某种魔性（daemonic）。他似乎什么也不回避：而这留给批评家们的作用，是令他们茫然失措。他们中间最敏锐的，只能纳罕一个平平常常的故事——譬如一些水手如何把船拖上滩、收了帆、走上岸、做了饭——究竟怎么会弄得这样富于诗意。他们倾向于把功劳在诗人与他的幸运时代之间对分——「那样的一个时代，」佩特（Pater）提示道，「人在其中简直难以开口而不产生理想的效果；水手们把小船放下水，也必成一幅『宏伟风格』的图画，衬着一片积满奇观的天空。」

===Original===
Well, the object of these lectures is not to explain genius. Just here it is rather to state a difficulty; to admit that, once in history, genius overcame it; yet warn you how rare in the tale of poetical achievement is such a success. Homer, indeed, stands first, if not unmatched, among poets in this technical triumph over the capital disability of annihilating flat passages. I omit Shakespeare and the dramatists; because they have only to give a stage direction “Enter Cassius, looking lean,” and Cassius comes in looking leaner than nature; whereas Homer has in his narrative to walk Hector or Thersites on to the scene, describe him, walk him off. I grant the rapidity of Dante. It is amazing; and we may yield him all the credit for choosing (it was his genius that chose it) a subject which allowed of the very highest rapidity; since Hell, Purgatory and Paradise, though they differ in other respects, have this in common, that they are populous and the inhabitants of each so compendiously shepherded together that the visitor can turn from one person to another without loss of time. But Homer does not escort us around a menagerie in which we can move expeditiously from one cage to another. He proposes at least, both in the *Iliad* and in the *Odyssey*, to unfold a story; and he *seems* to unfold it so artlessly that we linger on the most pedestrian intervals while he tells us, for example, what the heroes ate and how they cooked it. A modern writer would serve us a far better dinner. Homer brings us to his with our appetite all the keener for having waited and watched the spitting and roasting.

===Chinese===
喏，这一系列讲座的目的并不是解释天才。在这一点上，它毋宁是陈述一个困难；承认天才在历史上毕竟有一次克服了它；却又警告你们，在诗歌成就的史册里，这样的成功何其稀少。的确，在克服「消灭平淡段落」这一大难点的技术性胜利上，荷马纵非绝无仅有，也稳居诗人之首。我把莎士比亚和剧作家们撇开不论；因为他们只消下一句舞台指示「卡修斯上，面容消瘦」，卡修斯便上来了，而且比本色还要消瘦三分；荷马却得在自己的叙事里，把赫克托耳（Hector）或特尔西特斯（Thersites）领上台，描写他，再领他下去。我承认但丁（Dante）的迅疾。那真是惊人；我们尽可以把功劳全归给他，因为他选定了一个容许最高度迅疾的题材（正是他的天才做的这一选择）；既然地狱、炼狱与天堂尽管在其他方面各不相同，却有这样一点相同：它们都人烟稠密，而每一处的居民又被那么简捷地拢聚在一处，以至于访客从这一位转向那一位，毫不费时。可荷马并不领我们逛一座可以迅速从一笼移到另一笼的兽园。他至少打算——无论在《伊利亚特》（*Iliad*）还是《奥德赛》（*Odyssey*）里——铺陈一个故事；而他*显得*铺陈得那样毫无机心，以致我们在最平淡的段落上流连忘返，听他讲，比如说，英雄们吃了什么、又是怎么做的。一位现代作家会端给我们一顿好得多的饭。荷马却让我们先在旁边等一等、看着滴油翻烤，把胃口吊得足足的，然后才领我们上桌。

===Original===
I would point out to you what art this genius conceals; how cunning is this apparent simplicity: and for this purpose let me take Homer at the extreme of his difficulty—when he has to describe a long sea-voyage.

===Chinese===
我愿向你们指出，这天才掩藏着怎样的技艺；这显然的质朴之下，又藏着怎样的狡狯：为达此目的，让我把荷马放在他困难的最极端处来考察——当他不得不描写一次漫长海上航行的时候。

===Original===
Some years ago, in his last Oxford lectures, Mr. Froude lamented that no poet in this country had arisen to write a national epic of the great Elizabethan seamen, to culminate (I suppose) as his *History* culminated, in the defeat of the Armada: and one of our younger poets; Mr. Alfred Noyes, acting on this hint has since given us an epic poem on *Drake*, in twelve books. But Froude probably overlooked, as Mr. Noyes has not overcome, this difficulty of the flat interval which, while ever the bugbear of Epic, is magnified tenfold when our action takes place on the sea. For whereas the verse should be rapid and the high moments frequent, the business of seafaring is undeniably monotonous, as the intervals between port and port, sea-fight and sea-fight, must be long and lazy. Matters move more briskly in an occasional gale; but even a gale lasts, and must be ridden out; and the process of riding to a gale of wind:—

===Chinese===
几年前，弗劳德先生（Mr. Froude）在他最后一批牛津讲座里慨叹，本国始终没有出过一位诗人，来为伊丽莎白时代伟大的航海者写一部民族史诗，（我猜想）也像他的《历史》（*History*）那样，以无敌舰队（Armada）之败达到顶点：于是我们一位较年轻的诗人，阿尔弗雷德·诺伊斯先生（Mr. Alfred Noyes），循着这一提示，后来给我们拿出一部写《德雷克》（*Drake*）的史诗，凡十二卷。可是弗劳德大概忽略了、而诺伊斯先生也未能克服的，正是这平淡间隔的困难：它素来是史诗的梦魇，而当我们的行动发生在海上时，更要放大十倍。因为，诗理应迅疾，高昂时刻理应频仍，而航海这桩营生却无可否认地单调，港口与港口之间、海战与海战之间的间隔，必定漫长而懒散。偶起一场大风，事情倒进行得爽利些；可就连大风也有个完，须得顶过去；而顶风滞航这桩差事：——

===Original===
> Forever climbing up the climbing wave

===Chinese===
> 永远攀着那攀不尽的浪

===Original===
—your ship taking one wave much as she takes another—is in its nature monotonous. Nay, you have only to read Falconer’s *Shipwreck* to discover how much of dullness may lie enwrapped, to discharge itself, even in a first-class tempest. Courses, reckonings, trimmings of canvas—these occur in real life and amuse the simple mariner at the time. But to the reader, if he be a landsman, their repetition in narrative may easily become intolerable; and when we get down to the “trades,” even the seaman sets his sail for a long spell of weather and goes to sleep. In short you cannot upon the wide Atlantic push action and reaction to and fro as upon the plains of windy Troy: nor could any but a superhuman genius make sustained poetry (say) out of Nelson’s untiring pursuit of Villeneuve, which none the less was one of the most heroic feats in history.

===Chinese===
——你的船接一个浪头，同接下一个没什么两样——本质上就是单调的。不，你只消读一读法尔科纳（Falconer）的《沉船》（*Shipwreck*），就会发现：即便在一场头等的大风暴里，也可以裹藏多少沉闷、蓄势待发。航路、航迹推算、调帆——这些在真实生活里都会发生，当时也着实能让头脑简单的水手解闷。可对读者——倘若他是陆居的人——这些在叙事里的反复很容易变得忍无可忍；而一旦驶入「信风带」，连水手也要张起帆来应付一大段天气，自去睡觉了。简言之，在浩瀚的大西洋上，你无法像在多风的特洛伊平原上那样，把行动与反行动推来搡去；而纳尔逊（Nelson）对维尔纳夫（Villeneuve）那种不倦的追击——它终归是历史上最英勇的功业之一——除了一位超人的天才，谁也休想把它写成（比如说）持续不坠的诗。

===Original===
This difficulty, inherent in navigation as a subject for the Epic Muse, has, I think, been very shrewdly detected and hit off in a parody of Mr. Noyes’ poem by a young friend of mine, Mr. Wilfred Blair:—

===Chinese===
我想，这一内在于「以航海为史诗缪斯题材」的困难，已被我的一位年轻朋友威尔弗雷德·布莱尔先生（Mr. Wilfred Blair）在他戏仿诺伊斯先生诗作的一首谐诗里，非常精明地看破并且击中了：——

===Original===
> Meanwhile the wind had changed, and Francis Drake
 

 Put down the helm and drove against the seas—
 

 Once more the wind changed, and the simple seaman,
 

 Full fraught with weather wisdom, once again
 

 Put down the helm and so drove on—et cetera.

===Chinese===
> 这其间风又转了向，弗朗西斯·德雷克

压下舵轮，顶浪破海而驶——

风又一次转向，那纯朴的水手，

满腹观天的气象智慧，又一次

压下舵轮，径自驶去——如此等等。

===Original===
Now Homer actually has performed this feat which we declare to be next to impossible. He actually does convey Odysseus from Troy to Ithaca, by a ten years’ voyage too; he actually has narrated that voyage to us in plain straightforward words; and, what is more, he actually has made a superb epic of it. Yes, but when you come to dissect the Odyssey, what amazing artifice is found under that apparently straightforward tale!—eight years of the ten sliced out, to start with, and magnificently presented to Circe

===Chinese===
然而荷马当真完成了这桩我们宣称近乎不可能的绝技。他当真把奥德修斯（Odysseus）从特洛伊送到了伊萨卡（Ithaca），而且还是经由一场十年之久的航行；他当真用明白晓畅、直截了当的言语，把那次航行讲给了我们；不但如此，他还当真把它写成了一部华美的史诗。是，可是等到你解剖《奥德赛》，在那显然直陈的故事底下，会发现怎样惊人的匠心啊！——首先，十年一劈便砍去八年，而且大手笔地奉送给了喀耳刻（Circe）

===Original===
> Where that Aeaean isle forgets the main

===Chinese===
> 在那座埃埃亚岛忘却大海的地方

===Original===
—and (one may add), so forgetting, avoids the technical difficulties connected therewith.

===Chinese===
——而且（可以补一句），既这般忘却了，也就避开了随之而来的那些技术困难。

===Original===
Note the space given to Telemachus and his active search for the lost hero: note too how the mass of Odysseus’ seafaring adventures is condensed into a reported speech—a traveller’s tale at the court of Alcinoüs. Virgil borrowed this trick, you remember; and I dare to swear that had it fallen to Homer to attempt the impossible saga of Nelson’s pursuit after Villeneuve he would have achieved it triumphantly—by means of a tale told in the first person by a survivor to Lady Hamilton. Note, again, how boldly (being free to deal with an itinerary of which his audience knew nothing but surmised that it comprehended a vast deal of the marvellous, spaced at irregular distances) Homer works in a shipwreck or a miracle wherever the action threatens to flag. Lessing, as you know, devoted several pages of the *Laoköon* to the shield of Achilles; to Homer’s craft in depicting it as it grew under Hephaestus’ hammer: so that we are intrigued by the process of manufacture instead of being wearied by a description of the ready-made article; so also (if one may presume to add anything to Lessing) that we are cunningly flattered in a sense that the shield is being made for *us*. Well, that is one artifice out of many: but if you would gauge at all Homer’s resource and subtlety in technique I recommend you to analyse the first twelve books of the *Odyssey* and count for yourselves the device by which the poet—*πολὑτροπος* as was never his hero—evades or hurries over each flat interval as he happens upon it.

===Chinese===
请注意分给忒勒玛科斯（Telemachus）及其积极寻访失踪英雄的篇幅：也请注意，奥德修斯海上历险的大宗，是怎样被压缩进一段转述的话里——在阿尔基诺奥斯（Alcinoüs）宫廷里讲的一个旅人故事。维吉尔（Virgil）借用了这个诀窍，你们记得；我也敢起誓，倘若那桩不可能的差使——纳尔逊追击维尔纳夫的萨迦——落在荷马头上，他必定凯旋般地完成——用的法子，是让一名幸存者以第一人称向汉密尔顿夫人（Lady Hamilton）讲故事。再请注意，荷马（他自由支配着一条这样的航路——听众对它一无所知，只揣想沿途以不规律的间距散布着大宗奇观）何等大胆：凡行动有疲塌之虞处，便安插一场海难或一桩奇迹。莱辛（Lessing），如你们所知，在《拉奥孔》（*Laoköon*）里用了好几页写阿喀琉斯（Achilles）之盾；写荷马描写巨盾在赫菲斯托斯（Hephaestus）锤下渐渐成形的手笔：于是我们为制作过程本身着迷，而不是被一件现成货的描写弄得疲惫；也（倘若有人胆敢在莱辛之外有所增益）让我们在一层意思里被狡狯地恭维了：这盾原是为*我们*打的。喏，这是众多匠心之一：但你们若想多少掂一掂荷马技术上的富藏与精微，我劝你们去剖析《奥德赛》的前十二卷，亲自数一数诗人——*πολὑτροπος*（机变多端），远非他的主人公所能及——每逢撞上一个平淡的间隔，用的是些什么手法，将它闪避过去、或匆匆赶过去的。

===Original===
> These things, Ulysses,
 

 The wise bards also
 

 Behold and sing.
 

 But O, what labour!
 

 O Prince, what pain!

===Chinese===
> 这些事，尤利西斯，

智慧的歌手们

也目睹，也歌唱。

可是啊，何等辛劳！

王子啊，何等艰辛！

===Original===
You may be thinking, Gentlemen, that I take up a disproportionate amount of your time on such technical matters at these. But literature being an art (forgive the reiteration!) and therefore to be practised, I want us to be seeking all the time *how it is done*; to hunt out the principles on which the great artists wrought; to face, to rationalise, the difficulties by which they were confronted, and learn how they overcame the particular obstacle. Surely even for mere criticism, apart from practice, we shall equip ourselves better by seeking, so far as we may, how the thing is done than by standing at gaze before this or that masterpiece and murmuring “Isn’t that beautiful! How in the world, now…!”

===Chinese===
诸位先生，你们也许在想，我在诸如此类的技术问题上占用你们的时间，未免失之过多。但是文学既是一门艺术（恕我屡屡重申！），因而必须实践，我要我们时时刻刻都在探究*它是怎样做成的*；搜求伟大艺术家们据以工作的原理；直面他们遭遇的困难、把困难理清楚，并学习他们是怎样越过那一道道具体障碍的。可以肯定，哪怕单为批评计、撇开实践不谈，尽力去探求事情是怎样做成的，也比站在这一件那一件杰作面前瞠目呆望、喃喃念叨「这多美啊！这究竟是怎么弄的……！」更能把我们武装起来。

===Original===
I am told that these lectures are criticised as tending to make you conceited: to encourage in you a belief that you can do things, when it were better that you merely admired. Well I would not dishearten you by telling to what a shred of conceit, even of hope, a man can be reduced after twenty-odd years of the discipline. But I can, and do, affirm that the farther you penetrate in these discoveries the more sacred the ultimate mystery will become for you: that the better you understand the great authors as exemplars of practice, the more certainly you will realise what is the condescension of the gods.

===Chinese===
我听说，这些讲座被批评为有让你们自负的倾向：鼓励你们相信自己也能做成事情，而其实你们只消叹赏就好。唉，我不愿告诉你们，一个人经了这桩修炼二十多年之后，会缩减成怎样一丝自负、乃至一丝希望——免得叫你们灰心。但我能够、也确实断言：你们在这些探究里钻得越深，那终极的奥秘在你们眼中就会变得越神圣；你们越是把伟大作家们理解为实践的楷模，便越会确切地认识到，何谓诸神的俯就。

===Original===
Next time, then, we will attempt an enquiry into the capital difficulty of Prose.

===Chinese===
那么，下次我们将尝试探究散文的大难点。

## Lecture V Interlude: On Jargon / V 插叙：论套话

===Original===
Thursday, May 1

===Chinese===
5 月 1 日，星期四

===Original===
We parted, Gentlemen, upon a promise to discuss the capital difficulty of Prose, as we have discussed the capital difficulty of Verse. But, although we shall come to it, on second thoughts I ask leave to break the order of my argument and to interpose some words upon a kind of writing which, from a superficial likeness, commonly passes for prose in these days, and by lazy folk is commonly written for prose, yet actually is not prose at all; my excuse being the simple practical one that, by first clearing this sham prose out of the way, we shall the better deal with honest prose when we come to it. The proper difficulties of prose will remain: but we shall be agreed in understanding what it is, or at any rate what it is not, that we talk about. I remember to have heard somewhere of a religious body in the United States of America which had reason to suspect one of its churches of accepting spiritual consolation from a coloured preacher﻿—an offence against the laws of the Synod﻿—and despatched a Disciplinary Committee with power to act; and of the Committee’s returning to report itself unable to take any action under its terms of reference, for that while a person undoubtedly coloured had undoubtedly occupied the pulpit and had audibly spoken from it in the Committee’s presence, the performance could be brought within no definition of preaching known or discoverable. So it is with that infirmity of speech﻿—that flux, that determination of words to the mouth, or to the pen﻿—which, though it be familiar to you in parliamentary debates, in newspapers, and as the staple language of Blue Books, Committees, Official Reports, I take leave to introduce to you as prose which is not prose and under its real name of Jargon.

===Chinese===
诸位先生，我们上次分手时立下约定，要讨论散文（Prose）的大难点，一如我们已经讨论过的诗（Verse）的大难点。尽管我们终将讲到它，但再思之后，我请求允许打乱我论证的次序，插进几句，谈谈某一种文字——它凭着表面的相似，如今常被当作散文，懒人们也常把它当散文来写，可实际上根本不是散文；我的借口是一个简单而实际的理由：先把这种伪散文清扫出去，等我们真正讲到诚实的散文时，便能处理得更好。散文本身的种种难点依然会在：但至少我们将会商定一致，明白我们所谈论的是什么，或者无论如何，明白它不是什么。我记得在哪里听说过美利坚合众国的一个宗教团体，它有理由怀疑属下的一座教堂正在接受一位有色人种传道人的灵性慰藉——一桩触犯宗教会议（Synod）法规的过犯——于是派出一个握有行动权的纪律委员会；而委员会回来报告说，依照其职权范围，它无法采取任何行动：因为虽然一位毫无疑问是有色人种的人士，确实无疑地占用过讲坛，并且当着委员会的面从那里发出了听得见的话语，但这场表演无法纳入任何已知或可查明的布道定义之内。言语的这样一种痼疾——那种流泻、那种语词向口腔或向笔端的沉析——正是如此；它虽然在议会辩论里、在报纸上、作为蓝皮书（Blue Books）、委员会、官方报告的当家语言而为你们所熟悉，我仍要冒昧地把它介绍给你们，称之为并非散文的散文，并道出它的真名：套话（Jargon）。

===Original===
You must not confuse this Jargon with what is called Journalese. The two overlap, indeed, and have a knack of assimilating each other’s vices. But Jargon finds, maybe, the most of its votaries among good douce people who have never written to or for a newspaper in their life, who would never talk of “adverse climatic conditions” when they mean “bad weather”; who have never trifled with verbs such as “obsess,” “recrudesce,” “envisage,” “adumbrate,” or with phrases such as “the psychological moment,” “the true inwardness,” “it gives furiously to think.” It dallies with Latinity﻿—“*sub silentio*,” “*de die in diem*,” “*cui bono?*” (always in the sense, unsuspected by Cicero, of “What is the profit?”)﻿—but not for the sake of style. Your journalist at the worst is an artist in his way: he daubs paint of this kind upon the lily with a professional zeal; the more flagrant (or, to use his own word, arresting) the pigment, the happier is his soul. Like the Babu he is trying all the while to embellish our poor language, to make it more floriferous, more poetical﻿—like the Babu for example who, reporting his mother’s death, wrote, “Regret to inform you, the hand that rocked the cradle has kicked the bucket.”

===Chinese===
诸位切不可把这种套话与所谓新闻腔（Journalese）混为一谈。二者确有重叠，也各有沾染彼此恶习的本领。但套话的信众，多半倒落在一班安分守己（douce）的好人中间：他们平生从未给报纸写过一行字、也从未替报纸捉过刀，从不会把「坏天气」说成「不利气候条件」；从未玩弄过「着迷」（obsess）、「复燃」（recrudesce）、「设想」（envisage）、「勾勒」（adumbrate）一类动词，也没有玩弄过「心理时刻」、「真正的内蕴」、「这狠狠地发人深省」一类短语。套话也调弄拉丁风——*sub silentio*（默默之中）、*de die in diem*（日复一日）、*cui bono?*（于谁有利？）——总是取「有何益处？」之意，这层意思西塞罗（Cicero）是不曾梦见的——但并非为了风格的缘故。你们那位新闻记者，再不济也自有其一路艺术家的做派：他怀着职业的热忱，把这类颜料往百合花上涂抹；颜料愈是扎眼（或者用他自己的词说，愈是「抢眼」〔arresting〕），他的灵魂便愈是快活。他像巴布（Babu）一样，时时刻刻都在努力装点我们这可怜的语言，让它更繁花似锦、更富诗意——就像那位巴布，比方说，在讣告母亲的死讯时写道：「沉痛奉告：那只摇动摇篮的手，已经踢翻了水桶（kicked the bucket，俚语『蹬腿咽气』）。」

===Original===
*There* is metaphor: *there* is ornament: *there* is a sense of poetry, though as yet groping in a world unrealised. No such gusto marks﻿—no such zeal, artistic or professional, animates﻿—the practitioners of Jargon, who are, most of them (I repeat), douce respectable persons. Caution is its father: the instinct to save everything and especially trouble: its mother, Indolence. It looks precise, but it is not. It is, in these times, *safe*: a thousand men have said it before and not one to your knowledge had been prosecuted for it. And so, like respectability in Chicago, Jargon stalks unchecked in our midst. It is becoming the language of Parliament: it has become the medium through which Boards of Government, County Councils, Syndicates, Committees, Commercial Firms, express the processes as well as the conclusions of their thought and so voice the reason of their being.

===Chinese===
*那*才是隐喻（metaphor）：*那*才是藻饰：*那*才是一种诗感，尽管还在一个未曾实现的世界里摸索。套话的实践者身上却不见这种兴致——不见这种艺术的或职业的热忱在激励他们——这些人大多数（我重复一遍）是安分守己的可敬人士。它的父亲是谨慎：那种什么都想省、尤其想省麻烦的本能：它的母亲是怠惰。它看上去精确，其实并不。在我们这个时代，它是*保险的*：一千个人先前都这么说过，而据你们所知，没有一个人为此吃过官司。于是，正如体面在芝加哥畅行无阻一样，套话在我们中间昂首阔步、无人拦阻。它正在变成议会的语言：它已经变成政府各部门、郡议会、联合会、委员会、商业公司借以表达其思维之推演及其结论的媒介，从而道出其存在的理由。

===Original===
Has a Minister to say “No” in the House of Commons? Some men are constitutionally incapable of saying no: but the Minister conveys it thus﻿—“The answer to the question is in the negative.” That means “no.” Can you discover it to mean anything less, or anything more except that the speaker is a pompous person?﻿—which was no part of the information demanded.

===Chinese===
一位大臣要在下议院（House of Commons）说个「不」字吗？有些人天生就没有说「不」的本领：于是大臣这样传达它——「对该问题的答复是否定性的。」这话的意思是「不」。你们能发现它还有比这更少的含义吗？或者除了表明说话者是个浮夸之人以外，还有更多的含义吗？——而这可不是人家所要打听的内容的一部分。

===Original===
That is Jargon, and it happens to be accurate. But as a rule Jargon is by no means accurate, its method being to walk circumspectly around its target; and its faith, that having done so it has either hit the bull’s-eye or at least achieved something equivalent, and safer.

===Chinese===
那是套话，而且碰巧还算准确。但照例说来，套话绝不准确；它的方法，是绕着靶子小心翼翼地兜圈子；它的信仰则是：兜完了圈子，它要么已经击中靶心，要么至少取得了某种同等效验、而且更保险的东西。

===Original===
Thus the Clerk of a Board of Guardians will minute that﻿—

===Chinese===
于是，某济贫委员会（Board of Guardians）的文书会在记录簿上这样记道——

===Original===
> In the case of John Jenkins deceased the coffin provided was of the usual character.

===Chinese===
> 在已故约翰·詹金斯（John Jenkins）一案（case）中，所提供之棺材系属寻常性质（character）。

===Original===
Now this is not accurate. “In the case of John Jenkins deceased,” for whom a coffin was supplied, it is wholly superfluous to tell us that he is deceased. But actually John Jenkins never had more than one case, and that was the coffin. The Clerk says he had two﻿—a coffin in a case: but I suspect the Clerk to be mistaken, and I am sure he errs in telling us that the coffin was of the usual character: for coffins have no character, usual or unusual.

===Chinese===
这并不准确。「在已故约翰·詹金斯一案中」——棺材既是供应给他的，再告诉我们他已故世，纯属多余。可实际上，约翰·詹金斯一生从未有过第二个 case——若说有过一个，那也无非就是这口棺材。照文书的写法，他倒有两个——一个 case 里装着一口棺材：但我怀疑文书先生弄错了；而我可以肯定，他错在告诉我们那口棺材「系属寻常性质」：因为棺材根本无所谓性质，无论寻常与否。

===Original===
For another example (I shall not tell you whence derived)﻿—

===Chinese===
再举一例（出处我却不告诉你们）——

===Original===
> In the case of every candidate who is placed in the first class [So you see the lucky fellow gets a case as well as a first-class. He might be a stuffed animal: perhaps he is] In the case of every candidate who is placed in the first class the class-list will show by some convenient mark (1) the Section or Sections for proficiency in which he is placed in the first class and (2) the Section or Sections (if any) in which he has passed with special distinction.

===Chinese===
> 凡名列一等之候选人，皆有其 case［瞧，这位幸运儿得到一等之余，还得到一个 case。他没准儿是只剥制标本：说不定真是］凡名列一等之候选人，名单将以某种便利之标记 (1) 显示其以一等水平见录之部类或若干部类，及 (2) 其以特优成绩通过之部类或若干部类（如有）。

===Original===
“The Section or Sections (if any)”﻿—But, how, if they are not any, could they be indicated by a mark however convenient?

===Chinese===
「部类或若干部类（如有）」——可是，倘若压根儿「无」，那么无论标记何等便利，又怎能靠它把它们标示出来呢？

===Original===
> The Examiners will have regard to the style and method of the candidate’s answers, and will give credit for excellence *in these respects*.

===Chinese===
> 主考官将顾及考生答卷之风格与方法，并将就其*在这些方面*（in these respects）之优异给予嘉许。

===Original===
Have you begun to detect the two main vices of Jargon? The first is that it uses circumlocution rather than short straight speech. It says “In the case of John Jenkins deceased, the coffin” when it means “John Jenkins’s coffin”: and its yea is not yea, neither is its nay nay: but its answer is in the affirmative or in the negative, as the foolish and superfluous “case” may be. The second vice is that it habitually chooses vague woolly abstract nouns rather than concrete ones. I shall have something to say by-and-by about the concrete noun, and how you should ever be struggling for it whether in prose or in verse. For the moment I content myself with advising you, if you would write masculine English, never to forget the old tag of your Latin Grammar﻿—

===Chinese===
诸位可已开始察觉套话的两大恶习？第一，它用迂说（circumlocution）而不用简短径直的话。它想说「约翰·詹金斯的棺材」时，偏说「就已故约翰·詹金斯而言，那口棺材」：它的「是」不是是，它的「不是」也不是不是：它的答复只能是肯定性的或否定性的，端看那愚蠢而多余的「case」落在哪一头。第二，它惯于挑选含混的、羊毛团似的抽象名词（abstract noun），而不是具体名词（concrete noun）。关于具体名词，以及无论写散文还是写诗（verse），你们都该如何力争得到它，我稍后还有话要说。此刻我只想奉劝诸位：倘若你们想写阳刚的英语（masculine English），就千万不要忘记你们拉丁文法课本上的那句老歌诀——

===Original===
> Masculine will only be
 

 Things that you can touch and see.

===Chinese===
> 唯阳刚者，唯有那些
 

 你摸得到、看得见的东西。

===Original===
But since these lectures are meant to be a course in First Aid to writing, I will content myself with one or two extremely rough rules: yet I shall be disappointed if you do not find them serviceable.

===Chinese===
不过，既然这一系列讲座意在成为一门写作的急救课（First Aid），我便只满足于提出一两条极其粗略的规则：然而，倘若你们竟发现它们不切实用，我可要失望了。

===Original===
The first is:﻿—Whenever in your reading you come across one of these words, “case,” “instance,” “character,” “nature,” “condition,” “persuasion,” “degree”﻿—whenever in writing your pen betrays you to one or another of them﻿—pull yourself up and take thought. If it be “case” (I choose it as Jargon’s dearest child﻿—“in Heaven yclept Metonomy”) turn to the dictionary, if you will, and seek out what meaning can be derived from *casus*, its Latin ancestor: then try how, with a little trouble, you can extricate yourself from that case. The odds are, you will feel like a butterfly who has discarded his chrysalis.

===Chinese===
第一条是：——每当你们在阅读中撞见这几个词里的任何一个——「case（情形）」「instance（实例）」「character（性质）」「nature（本性）」「condition（状态）」「persuasion（信念）」「degree（程度）」——每当你们在写作中笔头一失足、被它们中的这一个或那一个绊住——就勒住自己，好好想一想。倘若是「case」（我选它，是因为它是套话最疼爱的孩子——「在天上名叫转喻〔Metonomy〕」），那么请去翻词典，若你愿意，查一查能从它的拉丁祖先 *casus* 里推衍出什么意义来：然后再试试，费上一点小事，你如何能从那个 case 里脱身出来。多半你会觉得，自己像一只蜕尽了蛹壳的蝴蝶。

===Original===
Here are some specimens to try your hand on﻿—
1. All those tears which inundated Lord Hugh Cecil’s head were dry in the case of Mr. Harold Cox.
Poor Mr. Cox! left gasping in his aquarium!
1. [From a cigar-merchant] In any case, let us send you a case on approval.
2. It is contended that Consols have fallen in consequence: but such is by no means the case.
“Such,” by the way, is another spoilt child of Jargon, especially in Committee’s Rules﻿—“Co-opted members may be eligible as such; such members to continue to serve for such time as”﻿—and so on.
1. Even in the purely Celtic areas, only in two or three cases do the Bishops bear Celtic names.
For “cases” read “dioceses.”

===Chinese===
这里有几件标本，供你们练手——
1. 那些淹没休·塞西尔勋爵（Lord Hugh Cecil）头顶的泪水，到了哈罗德·考克斯先生（Mr. Harold Cox）一案中却都干涸了。
可怜的考克斯先生！被撇在水族箱里直喘气！
1. [一位雪茄商来函] In any case（无论如何），且让我们寄上一 case（箱）供您试购。
2. 有人争辩说，统一公债（Consols）已因之下跌：但 such（此等情形）绝非 the case（事实）。
「Such」呢，顺便说一句，是套话的又一个被宠坏的孩子，在委员会章程里尤其如此——「增选之委员得因 such（这种）身份而具资格；such members（这种委员）须于 such time（这样的时限）内继续任职，如」——等等。
1. 即便在纯凯尔特（Celtic）地区，也只有两三个 cases（情形）中，各主教持有凯尔特名字。
这里的「cases」，应读作「dioceses（教区）」。

===Original===
> *Instance.* In most instances the players were below their form.

===Chinese===
> *Instance（实例）。* 在大多数实例（instances）中，球员们未达其应有状态。

===Original===
But what were they playing at? Instances?

===Chinese===
可他们打的是什么？打「实例」吗？

===Original===
> *Character﻿—Nature.* There can be no doubt that the accident was caused through the dangerous nature of the spot, the hidden character of the byroad, and the utter absence of any warning or danger signal.

===Chinese===
> *Character—Nature（性质—本性）。* 无可置疑，该事故之酿成，系由于该地点之危险本性（nature）、该岔路之隐蔽性质（character），以及任何警示或危险信号之全然阙如。

===Original===
Mark the foggy wording of it all! And yet the man hit something and broke his neck! Contrast that explanation with the verdict of a coroner’s jury in the West of England on a drowned postman﻿—“We find that deceased met his death by an act of God, caused by sudden overflowing of the river Walkhan and helped out by the scandalous neglect of the way-wardens.”

===Chinese===
请留意这整段措辞的雾气腾腾！然而那个人分明撞上了什么东西、折断了脖子！拿这份解释，对照一下英格兰西部一队验尸陪审团就一名溺亡邮递员所作的裁决——「我们认定：死者之丧生，乃出于上帝之作为（an act of God），系由沃尔肯河（river Walkhan）骤然泛滥所致，复因道路管理员骇人听闻之玩忽职守而促就。」

===Original===
> The Aintree course is notoriously of a trying nature.

===Chinese===
> 安特里（Aintree）赛道素以其性质（nature）磨人著称。

===Original===
> On account of its light character, purity and age, Usher’s whiskey is a whiskey that will agree with you.

===Chinese===
> 鉴于其性质（character）之轻和、纯净与陈年，厄舍（Usher's）威士忌乃一种能让阁下受用的威士忌。

===Original===
> *Order.* The mésalliance was of a pronounced order.

===Chinese===
> *Order（等类）。* 这桩 mésalliance（门第失称的联姻）属于显著的那一等类。

===Original===
> *Condition.* He was conveyed to his place of residence in an intoxicated condition.

===Chinese===
> *Condition（状态）。* 该员被移送至其居所时，处于一种 intoxicated condition（醉醺醺的状态）。

===Original===
“He was carried home drunk.”

===Chinese===
『他被人醉醺醺地抬回了家。』

===Original===
> *Quality and Section.* Mr. —﻿—﻿—, exhibiting no less than five works, all of a superior quality, figures prominently in the oil section.

===Chinese===
> *Quality and Section（品质与部类）。* 某君参展作品凡五件，均属上乘品质（superior quality），于油画部类（oil section）之中赫然在列。

===Original===
—This was written of an exhibition of pictures.

===Chinese===
——这段话写的是一场画展。

===Original===
> *Degree.* A singular degree of rarity prevails in the earlier editions of this romance.

===Chinese===
> *Degree（程度）。* 此小说之早期诸版本中，弥漫着一种非凡程度的稀见（a singular degree of rarity）。

===Original===
That is Jargon. In prose it runs simply “The earlier editions of this romance are rare”﻿—or “are very rare”﻿—or even (if you believe what I take leave to doubt), “are singularly rare”; which should mean that they are rarer than the editions of any other work in the world.

===Chinese===
这就是套话。用散文直说无非是：「这部小说的早期版本很稀见」——或者「非常稀见」——甚至（倘若你们信得过那一份我冒昧怀疑的自信）「稀罕得出奇」；而这最后一种说法，理应意味着它们比世上任何其他作品的版本都要稀见。

===Original===
Now what I ask you to consider about these quotations is that in each the writer was using Jargon to shirk prose, palming off periphrases upon us when with a little trouble he could have gone straight to the point. “A singular degree of rarity prevails,” “the accident was caused through the dangerous nature of the spot,” “but such is by no means the case.” We may not be capable of much; but we can all write better than that, if we take a little trouble. In place of, “the Aintree course is of a trying nature” we can surely say “Aintree is a trying course” or “the Aintree course is a trying one”﻿—just that and nothing more.

===Chinese===
现在，我请诸位就这些引文考虑一点：在每一例中，写作者都在用套话逃避散文，拿迂说（periphrasis）来蒙混我们，其实只要稍微费点事，他本可以直截了当说到点子上。「弥漫着一种非凡程度的稀见」、「事故系由于该地点之危险本性」、「但此等情形绝非事实」。我们的本事也许有限；但只要稍费点事，我们谁都能写得比那好。与其说「安特里赛道素以其性质磨人著称」，我们满可以说「安特里是条磨人的赛道」，或者「安特里赛道是磨人的」——恰恰如此，不多一字。

===Original===
Next, having trained yourself to keep a lookout for these worst offenders (and you will be surprised to find how quickly you get into the way of it), proceed to push your suspicions out among the whole cloudy host of abstract terms. “How excellent a thing is sleep,” sighed Sancho Panza; “it wraps a man round like a cloak”﻿—an excellent example, by the way, of how to say a thing concretely: a Jargoneer would have said that “among the beneficent qualities of sleep its capacity for withdrawing the human consciousness from the contemplation of immediate circumstances may perhaps be accounted not the least remarkable.” How vile a thing﻿—shall we say?﻿—is the abstract noun! It wraps a man’s thoughts round like cotton wool.

===Chinese===
其次，在训练自己时刻提防这批最恶劣的惯犯之后（你们会惊讶地发现自己多么快就养成了这个习惯），再把你们的怀疑推向抽象名词（abstract terms）那整整一支云雾茫茫的大军。「睡眠这东西何其美妙，」桑丘·潘沙（Sancho Panza）叹道，「它像一件斗篷把人整个裹住」——顺便说一句，这是如何把一件事说得具体的一个绝好范例：换一位套话家，就会说「在睡眠之种种有益品质当中，其将人类意识自对当下处境之观照中撤离之能力，或可视为并非最不显著者」。抽象名词这东西是何等可恶——我们可以这样说吧！——它像药棉一样把人的思想裹了个严实。

===Original===
Here is a pretty little nest of specimens, found in *The Times* newspaper by Messrs. H. W. and F. G. Fowler, authors of that capital little book *The King’s English*:﻿—

===Chinese===
这里有一小窝漂亮的标本，是 H. W. 福勒与 F. G. 福勒两位先生（Messrs. H. W. and F. G. Fowler）——那本出色的小书《国王的英语》（*The King's English*）的作者——在《泰晤士报》（*The Times*）上找到的：——

===Original===
> One of the most important reforms mentioned in the rescript is the unification of the organisation of judicial institutions and the guarantee for all the tribunals of the independence necessary for securing to all classes of the community equality before the law.

===Chinese===
> 诏书所提最重要的改革之一，是司法机构组织之统一化，以及为所有法庭保障其独立性——此项独立性，乃为确保社会各阶层在法律面前之平等所必需者。

===Original===
I do not dwell on the cacophony; but, to convey a straightforward piece of news, might not the Editor of *The Times* as well employ a man to write:﻿—

===Chinese===
我不去细究它的拗口（cacophony）；但为传递一段直截了当的新闻，《泰晤士报》的主编满可以雇一个人这样来写：——

===Original===
> One of the most important reforms is that of the Courts, which need a uniform system and to be made independent. In this way only can men be assured that all are equal before the law.

===Chinese===
> 最重要的改革之一在于法院：法院需要一套统一的体制，并需要取得独立。唯有如此，人们方能确信人人在法律面前平等。

===Original===
I think he might.
A day or two ago the musical critic of the *Standard* wrote this:﻿—

===Chinese===
我想他满可以。
一两天前，《旗帜报》（*Standard*）的乐评人写了这么一段：——

===Original===
> Mr. Frederick Lamond, the Scottish pianist, as an interpreter of Beethoven has few rivals. At his second recital of the composer’s works at Bechstein Hall on Saturday afternoon he again displayed a complete sympathy and understanding of his material that extracted the very essence of aesthetic and musical value from each selection he undertook. The delightful intimacy of his playing and his unusual force of individual expression are invaluable assets, which, allied to his technical brilliancy, enable him to achieve an artistic triumph. The two lengthy Variations in E flat major (Op. 35) and in D major, the latter on the Turkish March from *The Ruins of Athens*, when included in the same programme, require a master hand to provide continuity of interest. *To say that Mr. Lamond successfully avoided moments that might at times, in these works, have inclined to comparative disinterestedness, would be but a moderate way of expressing the remarkable fascination with which his versatile playing endowed them*, but *at the same time* two of the sonatas given included a similar form of composition, and no matter how intellectually brilliant may be the interpretation, the extravagant use of a certain mode is bound in time to become somewhat ineffective. In the Three Sonatas, the E major (Op. 109), the A major (Op. 2), No. 2, and the C minor (Op. 111), Mr. Lamond signalised his perfect insight into the composer’s varying moods.

===Chinese===
> 苏格兰钢琴家弗雷德里克·拉蒙德先生（Mr. Frederick Lamond），作为贝多芬（Beethoven）的诠释者，鲜有敌手。星期六下午，他在贝希斯坦音乐厅（Bechstein Hall）举行的本作曲家作品第二场独奏会上，再次显示出对其素材的完全的同情与理解，从他所承担的每一首曲目中，萃取了审美价值与音乐价值的精华。他演奏中可喜的亲切感与他个人表现的不寻常力量，都是宝贵的资产，连同他技巧的光辉，使他得以成就一场艺术的凯旋。降 E 大调与 D 大调两首冗长的变奏曲（前者为作品第 35 号，后者据《雅典的废墟》（*The Ruins of Athens*）中的土耳其进行曲），当二者编入同一节目时，需要一只大师之手来维持趣味的连贯。*若说拉蒙德先生在这些作品里成功地避开了那些时而或许会流于相对淡漠的时刻，那不过是对他那多才多艺的演奏赋予它们的非凡魅力的一种温和说法罢了*，但*与此同时*，所奏的两首奏鸣曲包含了同一形式的创作，而不论诠释在智性上何等辉煌，对某一模式的挥霍使用，迟早会变得有点失效。在三首奏鸣曲——E 大调（作品第 109 号）、A 大调（作品第 2 号之 2）与 C 小调（作品第 111 号）——中，拉蒙德先生标示出他对作曲家种种心境的完美洞见。

===Original===
Will you not agree with me that here is no writing, here is no prose, here is not even English, but merely a flux of words to the pen?

===Chinese===
诸位岂不同意我的看法：这里没有写作，这里没有散文，这里甚至没有英语，有的只是一场向笔端倾泻的词流？

===Original===
Here again is a string, a concatenation﻿—say, rather, a tiara﻿—of gems of purest ray serene from the dark unfathomed caves of a Scottish newspaper:﻿—

===Chinese===
这里又是一串、一连——不如说，是一顶冠冕——由最纯净静穆之光芒的宝石缀成，采自一家苏格兰报纸幽暗而莫测的洞穴：——

===Original===
> The Chinese viewpoint, as indicated in this letter, may not be without interest to your readers, because it evidently is suggestive of more than an academic attempt to explain an unpleasant aspect of things which, if allowed to materialise, might suddenly culminate in disaster resembling the Chang-Sha riots. It also ventures to illustrate incidents having their inception in recent premature endeavours to accelerate the development of Protestant missions in China; but we would hope for the sake of the interests involved that what my correspondent describes as “the irresponsible ruffian element” may be known by their various religious designations only within very restricted areas.

===Chinese===
> 本函所表明的中国观点（viewpoint），于贵报读者或不无趣味，因其显然暗示了某种不止于学理尝试的东西，用以解释事物之一个不愉快的方面；此一方面倘听任其成形（materialise），或会遽然臻于一场类似长沙骚乱（the Chang-Sha riots）的灾祸。本函亦斗胆举例说明若干事件；此等事件发端于近来为加速新教在华传教事业之发展而作的种种为时尚早的努力；但为有关各方之利益计，我们惟愿：我通信者口中的「不负责任的暴徒分子」，其得以各种宗教名号为人所知者，仅限于极为有限的地区之内。

===Original===
Well, the Chinese have given it up, poor fellows! and are asking the Christians﻿—as today’s newspapers inform us﻿—to pray for them. Do you wonder? But that is, or was, the Chinese “viewpoint,”﻿—and what a willow-pattern viewpoint! Observe its delicacy. It does not venture to interest or be interesting; merely “to be not without interest.” But it does “venture to illustrate incidents”﻿—which, for a viewpoint, is brave enough: and this illustration “is suggestive of something more than an academic attempt to explain an unpleasant aspect of things which, if allowed to materialise, might suddenly culminate.” What materialises? The unpleasant aspect? or the things? Grammar says the “things,” “things which if allowed to materialise.” But things are materialised already, and as a condition of their being things. It must be the aspect, then, that materialises. But, if so, it is also the aspect that culminates, and an aspect, however unpleasant, can hardly do that, or at worst cannot culminate in anything resembling the Chang-Sha riots.﻿ ﻿… I give it up.

===Chinese===
唉，中国人已经放弃啦，可怜的人们！而且——今日报纸如此见告——正在请求基督徒为他们祈祷。你们奇怪吗？但这便是，或者说曾是，中国的「观点」——何等柳纹瓷式（willow-pattern）的观点！请留意它的纤巧。它不敢斗胆去引人兴趣、或使自己有趣；只敢「不无趣味」（to be not without interest）。但它确实「斗胆举例说明若干事件」——就一个观点而言，这算是够勇敢的了：而这举例说明，「暗示了某种不止于学理尝试的东西，用以解释事物之一个不愉快的方面；此一方面倘听任其成形，或会遽然臻于」。是什么成形（materialise）？是那不愉快的方面？还是那些事物？语法规矩说，是「事物」，「那些事物，倘听任其成形」。可事物早已经成形了，而且是以其身为事物为前提的。那么，成形的必定是那个方面了。但倘若如此，遽然臻于顶点的也是那个方面；而一个方面，无论多么不愉快，很难干得了这个，或者退一万步说，也臻不出任何与长沙骚乱相似的东西来。  ……我放弃。

===Original===
Let us turn to another trick of Jargon: the trick of Elegant Variation, so rampant in the Sporting Press that there, without needing to attend these lectures, the undergraduate detects it for laughter:﻿—

===Chinese===
让我们转向套话的另一套把戏：雅致变换（Elegant Variation）的把戏。它在体育报刊上如此横行，以致在那里，本科生无需来上这些讲座便能察觉它、讥笑它：——

===Original===
> Hayward and C. B. Fry now faced the bowling; which apparently had no terrors for the Surrey crack. The old Oxonian, however, took some time in settling to work.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
> 海沃德（Hayward）与 C. B. 弗莱（C. B. Fry）此刻迎击投球；这投球对那位萨里队（Surrey）的顶尖好手似乎毫无威慑。然而那位老牛津人（Oxonian）还是过了一阵子才安顿下来进入状态。……

===Original===
Yes, you all recognise it and laugh at it. But why do you practise it in your essays? An undergraduate brings me an essay on Byron. In an essay on Byron, Byron is (or ought to be) mentioned many times. I expect, nay exact, that Bryon shall be mentioned again and again. But my undergraduate has a blushing sense that to call Byron Byron twice on one page is indelicate. So Byron, after starting bravely as Byron, in the second sentence turns into “that great but unequal poet” and thenceforward I have as much trouble with Byron as ever Telemachus with Proteus to hold and pin him back to his proper self. Halfway down the page he becomes “the gloomy master of Newstead”: overleaf he is reincarnated into “the meteoric darling of society”: and so proceeds through successive avatars﻿—“this arch-rebel,” “the author of *Childe Harold*,” “the apostle of scorn,” “the ex-Harrovian, proud, but abnormally sensitive of his clubfoot,” “the martyr of Missolonghi,” “the pageant-monger of a bleeding heart.” Now this again is Jargon. It does not, as most Jargon does, come of laziness; but it comes of timidity, which is worse. In literature as in life he makes himself felt who not only calls a spade a spade but has the pluck to double spades and redouble.

===Chinese===
是的，你们全都认得出它，也都在笑话它。可你们为什么又在自己文章里实践起它来呢？一位本科生交给我一篇论拜伦的文章。在一篇论拜伦的文章里，拜伦（Byron）是（或者说理应）被提到许多次的。我期待——不，我要求——拜伦被一而再、再而三地提到。可我这位本科生却带着一层羞红，觉得在一页纸上把拜伦叫作拜伦两次，未免失于不雅。于是拜伦起先还勇敢地以拜伦之名登场，到第二句便摇身变成「那位伟大而不均衡的诗人」，从此我侍候这位拜伦，就像当年忒勒玛科斯（Telemachus）侍候普罗透斯（Proteus），要费尽气力捉住他、把他钉回他的本来面目。到半页之下，他成了「纽斯特德（Newstead）的阴郁主人」；翻过一页，他又转世为「社交界那颗流星般的宠儿」；如此历尽一连串化身——「这位大叛逆者」「《恰尔德·哈罗尔德游记》（*Childe Harold*）的作者」「轻蔑的使徒」「那位哈罗公学旧生，骄傲，却对畸形足异常敏感」「迈索隆吉（Missolonghi）的殉道者」「那颗流血之心的大典贩子」。这又是一种套话。它不像大多数套话那样出于怠惰；而是出于怯懦——这更糟。在文学里如同在生活中，真正让人感到分量的，是那种不仅把铲子叫作铲子、而且有胆量把黑桃加倍再加倍的人。

===Original===
For another rule﻿—just as rough and ready, but just as useful: Train your suspicions to bristle up whenever you come upon “as regards,” “with regard to,” “in respect of,” “in connection with,” “according as to whether,” and the like. They are all dodges of Jargon, circumlocutions for evading this or that simple statement: and I say that it is not enough to avoid them nine times out of ten, or nine-and-ninety times out of a hundred. You should never use them. That is positive enough, I hope? Though I cannot admire his style, I admire the man who wrote to me, “Re Tennyson﻿—your remarks anent his *In Memoriam* make me sick”: for though “re” is not a preposition of the first water, and “anent” has enjoyed its day, the finish crowned the work. But here are a few specimens far, very far, worse:﻿—

===Chinese===
再给一条规则——同样地粗率而现成，却同样地有用：训练你们的疑心，让它们一碰到「as regards（关于）」「with regard to（至于）」「in respect of（就……而言）」「in connection with（与……相关连）」「according as to whether（须视是否……而定）」以及诸如此类，就根根竖起。它们全是套话的伎俩，全是为逃避这个或那个简单陈述而设的迂说：而我要说，十回里躲开它们九回，或者一百回里躲开九十九回，都是不够的。你们应当一回也不用。这话够斩钉截铁了吧，我希望？尽管我不敢恭维他的风格，我却佩服那位给我来信的人，他写道：「Re：丁尼生（Tennyson）——阁下 anent（关于）其《悼念集》（*In Memoriam*）的高论令在下作呕（make me sick）」：因为尽管「re」算不上头一等的介词，「anent」也早经风光过它的年代，可这个收尾给全文加了冕。但这里有几件还要糟得远、糟得远的标本：——

===Original===
> The special difficulty in Professor Minocelsi’s case [our old friend “case” again] arose *in connection with* the view he holds *relative to* the historical value of the opening pages of Genesis.

===Chinese===
> 米诺切尔西教授（Professor Minocelsi）一案的特殊困难[我们的老朋友「case」又来了]，系 *in connection with*（关连于）他所持*relative to*（相对于）《创世记》（Genesis）开篇诸页之历史价值的见解而产生。

===Original===
That is Jargon. In prose, even taking the miserable sentence as it stands constructed, we should write “the difficulty arose over the views he holds about the historical value,” etc.

===Chinese===
这是套话。用散文写——即便就照这句可怜句子现成的构造——我们也该写「困难起于他就历史价值所持的见解」，等等。

===Original===
From a popular novelist:﻿—

===Chinese===
出自一位流行小说家：——

===Original===
> I was entirely indifferent *as to* the results of the game, caring nothing at all *as to* whether *I had losses or gains*﻿—

===Chinese===
> 我对于比赛之结果全然漠然，*as to*（至若）我究竟*是蒙受损失还是有所进益*，亦毫不在意——

===Original===
Cut out the first “as” in “as to,” and the second “as to” altogether, and the sentence begins to be prose﻿—“I was indifferent to the results of the game, caring nothing whether I had losses or gains.”

===Chinese===
把第一个「as to」里的「as」删掉，把第二个「as to」整个删掉，这句话就开始成其为散文了——「我对比赛的结果漠不关心，无论输赢都不在乎」。

===Original===
But why, like Dogberry, have “had losses”? Why not simply “lose.” Let us try again. “I was entirely indifferent to the results of the game, caring nothing at all whether I won or lost.”

===Chinese===
可是为什么像道格培里（Dogberry）那样说「had losses（蒙受损失）」呢？何不干脆说「输（lose）」。让我们再试一遍。「我对比赛的结果全然漠然，无论输赢都毫不在乎。」

===Original===
Still the sentence remains absurd: for the second clause but repeats the first without adding one jot. For if you care not at all whether you win or lose, you must be entirely indifferent to the results of the game. So why not say “I was careless if I won or lost,” and have done with it?

===Chinese===
这句话依旧荒唐：因为第二个分句不过是把第一个分句重复了一遍，一星半点也没有增添。因为，倘若你们对输赢毫不在乎，那你们对比赛的结果必然全然漠然。那么何不说「输也罢赢也罢，我都无所谓」，就此拉倒？

===Original===
> A man of simple and charming character, he was fitly *associated with* the distinction of the Order of Merit.

===Chinese===
> 某君性情淳朴可爱，恰如其分地*与*功绩勋章（the Order of Merit）*之荣耀相关联（associated with）*。

===Original===
I take this gem with some others from a collection made three years ago, by the *Oxford Magazine*; and I hope you admire it as one beyond price. “He was associated with the distinction of the Order of Merit” means “he was given the Order of Merit.” If the members of that Order make a society then he was associated with them; but you cannot associate a man with a distinction. The inventor of such fine writing would doubtless have answered Canning’s Needy Knife-grinder with:﻿—

===Chinese===
这颗宝石，连同另外几颗，我采自《牛津杂志》（*Oxford Magazine*）三年前辑成的一个集子；我希望你们激赏它是无价之品。「他与功绩勋章之荣耀相关联」，意思是「他被授予了功绩勋章」。倘若该勋章的受勋者们组成一个会社，那么说「他与他们相关联」倒也成立；但你无法把一个人与一项荣誉「相关联」起来。此种妙文的发明者，毫无疑问，会用这样的话去回答坎宁（Canning）笔下的穷磨刀匠：——

===Original===
> I associate thee with sixpence! I will see thee in another association first!

===Chinese===
> 我愿你与六便士相联（associate）！我得先在另一种关联（association）里见到你再说！

===Original===
But let us close our *florilegium* and attempt to illustrate Jargon by the converse method of taking a famous piece of English (say Hamlet’s soliloquy) and remoulding a few lines of it in this fashion:﻿—

===Chinese===
但让我们合上这部*采英集*（*florilegium*），试用相反之法来说明套话：取一段著名的英文（譬如哈姆雷特〔Hamlet〕的独白），把它的几行按下面这种方式重铸一遍：——

===Original===
> To be, or the contrary? Whether the former or the latter be preferable would seem to admit of some difference of opinion; the answer in the present case being of an affirmative or of a negative character according as to whether one elects on the one hand to mentally suffer the disfavour of fortune, albeit in an extreme degree, or on the other to boldly envisage adverse conditions in the prospect of eventually bringing them to a conclusion. The condition of sleep is similar to, if not indistinguishable from, that of death; and with the addition of finality the former might be considered identical with the latter: so that in this connection it might be argued with regard to sleep that, could the addition be effected, a termination would be put to the endurance of a multiplicity of inconveniences, not to mention a number of downright evils incidental to our fallen humanity, and thus a consummation achieved of a most gratifying nature.

===Chinese===
> 生存，抑或相反？前者与后者孰为可取，似可容许有若干意见之分歧；就目前情形而论，答复具有肯定或否定之性质，须视当事人系选择一方面在精神上承受命运之失宠（ albeit 达于极端之程度），抑或另一方面大胆设想种种不利之条件、以期终将上述条件引至终结而定。睡眠之状态，如非与死亡无从分辨，亦与之相似；且若附加终局性一项，前者或可被认为与后者同一：故就此关联而言，或可就睡眠提出如下主张：倘上述附加能够实现，则对为数众多之种种不便之忍受将告终止，更遑论若干直截了当之罪恶——凡此皆为我堕落之人性所固有——并从而达成一项性质上至为可喜之圆满。

===Original===
That is Jargon: and to write Jargon is to be perpetually shuffling around in the fog and cotton-wool of abstract terms; to be forever hearkening, like Ibsen’s Peer Gynt, to the voice of the Boyg exhorting you to circumvent the difficulty, to beat the air because it is easier than to flesh your sword in the thing. The first virtue, the touchstone of a masculine style, is its use of the active verb and the concrete noun. When you write in the active voice, “They gave him a silver teapot,” you write as a man. When you write “He was made the recipient of a silver teapot,” you write jargon. But at the beginning set even higher store on the concrete noun. Somebody﻿—I think it was FitzGerald﻿—once posited the question, “What would have become of Christianity if Jeremy Bentham had had the writing of the Parables?” Without pursuing that dreadful enquiry I ask you to note how carefully the Parables﻿—those exquisite short stories﻿—speak only of “things which you can touch and see”﻿—“A sower went forth to sow,” “The kingdom of heaven is like unto leaven, which a woman took,”﻿—and not the Parables only, but the Sermon on the Mount and almost every verse of the Gospel. The Gospel does not, like my young essayist, fear to repeat a word, if the word be good. The Gospel says “Render unto Caesar the things that are Caesar’s”﻿—not “Render unto Caesar the things that appertain to that potentate.” The Gospel does not say “Consider the growth of the lilies,” or even “Consider how the lilies grow.” It says, “Consider the lilies, how they grow.”

===Chinese===
那就是套话：写套话，就是永远在抽象名词的雾气和药棉里拖着脚步打转；就是永远在听命，像易卜生（Ibsen）的《培尔·金特》（Peer Gynt）里那样，听波伊格（the Boyg）的声音劝你绕开困难、向空气挥剑，因为那比把剑刺进实物容易。阳刚风格（masculine style）的第一美德、它的试金石，是对主动动词（active verb）与具体名词的运用。当你用主动语态写「他们送了他一把银茶壶」时，你是在以一个男子汉的身份写作。当你写「他被置为一把银茶壶之接受者（recipient）」时，你写的是套话。但在起初，你们更要把更高的赌注押在具体名词上。有个人——我想是菲茨杰拉德（FitzGerald）——曾经提出过这样一个问题：「倘若耶稣的比喻（Parables）是由杰里米·边沁（Jeremy Bentham）执笔的，基督教会变成什么样子？」不去追究那个可怕的追问吧，我只请诸位留意，那些比喻——那些精妙的短篇故事——是何等谨慎地只说「你摸得到、看得见的事物」——「有一个撒种的出去撒种」，「天国好像面酵，有妇人拿来」，——而且不止是比喻，还有登山宝训（Sermon on the Mount），以及福音书里几乎每一节经文。福音书不像我那位年轻的文章家，它不害怕重复一个词，只要那词是好词。福音书说「凯撒的物当归给凯撒」——而不说「凡归属于那位权贵大人的物，当归给那位权贵大人」。福音书不说「想想百合花的生长」，甚至也不说「想想百合花怎样生长」。它说的是：「想想百合花吧，看它们怎样长起来。」

===Original===
Or take Shakespeare. I wager you that no writer of English so constantly chooses the concrete word, in phrase after phrase forcing you to touch and see. No writer so insistently teaches the general through the particular. He does it even in “Venus and Adonis” (as Professor Wendell, of Harvard, pointed out in a brilliant little monograph on Shakespeare, published some ten years ago). Read any page of “Venus and Adonis” side by side with any page of Marlowe’s “Hero and Leander” and you cannot but mark the contrast: in Shakespeare the definite, particular, visualised image, in Marlowe the beautiful generalisation, the abstract term, the thing seen at a literary remove. Take the two openings, both of which start out with the sunrise. Marlowe begins:﻿—

===Chinese===
或者拿莎士比亚（Shakespeare）来看。我敢同你们打赌，没有哪位英语作家如此一贯地挑选具体的词，一个短语接一个短语地逼着你去触摸、去观看。没有哪位作家如此执拗地以特殊教人领会一般。甚至在《维纳斯与阿多尼》（Venus and Adonis）里，他也是这样做的（这一点，哈佛的温德尔教授〔Professor Wendell, of Harvard〕在大约十年前出版的一部论莎士比亚的精彩小专著里指出过）。把《维纳斯与阿多尼》的任何一页与马洛（Marlowe）的《希罗与利安德》（Hero and Leander）的任何一页并排对读，你们不能不注意到那对照：在莎士比亚是确切的、特殊的、历历可见的形象，在马洛是漂亮的概括、抽象的名词、隔着一层文学距离看到的事物。且拿两家的开头来看——两者都从日出写起。马洛起笔道：——

===Original===
> Now had the Morn espied her lover’s steeds:
 

 Whereat she starts, puts on her purple weeds,
 

 And, red for anger that he stay’d so long,
 

 All headlong throws herself the clouds among.

===Chinese===
> 晨光此刻已窥见她情人的骏马：
 

 她一惊，慌忙披上紫红的衫裙，
 

 又因他流连太久恼得满面通红，
 

 一个倒栽葱便扎进了云堆深处。

===Original===
Shakespeare wastes no words on Aurora and her feelings, but gets to his hero and to business without ado:﻿—

===Chinese===
莎士比亚在奥罗拉（Aurora）和她的感受上不置一词，直奔他的主人公、直奔正题，毫不耽搁：——

===Original===
> Even as the sun with purple-colour’d face﻿—

===Chinese===
> 正如那太阳，带着紫红的脸庞——

===Original===
(You have the sun visualised at once),

===Chinese===
（太阳顿时已在你们眼前），

===Original===
> Even as the sun with purple-colour’d face
 

 Had ta’en his last leave of the weeping morn,
 

 Rose-cheek’d Adonis hied him to the chase;
 

 Hunting he loved, but love he laugh’d to scorn.

===Chinese===
> 正如那太阳，带着紫红的脸庞
 

 已向垂泪的晨光诀别了最后一遭，
 

 双颊绯红的阿多尼（Adonis）赶去行猎；
 

 行猎他热爱，爱情他却付之一哂。

===Original===
When Shakespeare has to describe a horse, mark how definite he is:﻿—

===Chinese===
而当莎士比亚不得不描写一匹马时，请留意他写得何等确切：——

===Original===
> Round-hoof’d, short-jointed, fetlocks shag and long,
 

 Broad breast, full eye, small head and nostril wide,
 

 High crest, short ears, straight legs and passing strong;
 

 Thin mane, thick tail, broad buttock, tender hide.

===Chinese===
> 蹄圆，节短，距毛粗而长，
 

 胸宽，眼饱，头小鼻孔张，
 

 项高，耳短，腿直劲道强；
 

 鬣薄，尾丰，臀阔皮质细嫩。

===Original===
Or again, in a casual simile, how definite:﻿—

===Chinese===
或者再看一处信手拈来的明喻（simile），又是何等确切：——

===Original===
> Upon this promise did he raise his chin,
 

 Like a dive-dipper peering through a wave,
 

 Which, being look’d on, ducks as quickly in.

===Chinese===
> 听了这许诺他才抬起下巴，
 

 像一只潜水的河乌从浪里窥看，
 

 一被人瞧见，便又急忙钻回浪间。

===Original===
Or take, if you will, Marlowe’s description of Hero’s first meeting Leander:﻿—

===Chinese===
或者，倘若你们愿意，取马洛对希罗（Hero）初遇利安德（Leander）的描写来看：——

===Original===
> It lies not in our power to love or hate,
 

 For will in us is overruled by fate﻿ ﻿… ,

===Chinese===
> 爱与恨都不由我们的权力做主，
 

 因为我们身上的意志被命运压服……，

===Original===
and set against it Shakespeare’s description of Venus’ last meeting with Adonis, as she came on him lying in his blood:﻿—

===Chinese===
再拿莎士比亚对维纳斯（Venus）与阿多尼最后一面的描写来同它对照——她撞见他躺在血泊之中：——

===Original===
> Or as a snail whose tender horns being hit
 

 Shrinks backward in his shelly cave with pain,
 

 And there, all smother’d up, in shade doth sit,
 

 Long after fearing to creep forth again;
 

 So, at his bloody view﻿—

===Chinese===
> 又像一只蜗牛，柔嫩的触角被击中，
 

 便疼得缩回它壳中的洞穴，
 

 在那里，蜷作一团，坐在浓荫底下，
 

 许久许久不敢再往外爬；
 

 她，一见他那血淋淋的模样，也如此——

===Original===
I do not deny Marlowe’s lines (if you will study the whole passage) to be lovely. You may even judge Shakespeare’s to be crude by comparison. But you cannot help noting that whereas Marlowe steadily deals in abstract, nebulous terms, Shakespeare constantly uses concrete ones, which later on he learned to pack into verse, such as:﻿—

===Chinese===
我并不否认马洛的这两行（倘若你们研读全段）是美的。两相对照，你们甚至可能断定莎士比亚的显得粗拙。但你们不能不注意到：马洛一以贯之地经营抽象的、星云般的词，莎士比亚却不断地使用具体的词——这些具体的词，他后来还学会了如何压进诗行（verse），例如：——

===Original===
> Sleep that knits up the ravell’d sleeve of care.

===Chinese===
> 睡眠，它把忧虑的绽线纱袖织补如初。

===Original===
Is it unfair to instance Marlowe, who died young? Then let us take Webster for the comparison; Webster, a man of genius or of something very like it, and commonly praised by the critics for his mastery over definite, detailed, and what I may call *solidified sensation*. Let us take this admired passage from his *Duchess of Malfi*:﻿—
How doth our sister Duchess bear herself
 

 In her imprisonment?
Nobly: I’ll describe her.
 

 She’s sad as one long used to ’t, and she seems
 

 Rather to welcome the end of misery
 

 Than shun it: a behaviour so noble
 

 As gives a majesty to adversity3
 

 You may discern the shape of loveliness
 

 More perfect in her tears than in her smiles;
 

 She will muse for hours together;4 and her silence
 

 Methinks expresseth more than if she spake.

===Chinese===
拿早逝的马洛来作例证，是否有失公允？那么，让我们改取韦伯斯特（Webster）来作对照。韦伯斯特，一个有天才的人——或者有某种极近乎天才的东西——素来被批评家们称道，说他驾驭得住确切的、细致的、以及我可称之为*凝固的感觉*（solidified sensation）的东西。让我们取《马尔菲公爵夫人》（Duchess of Malfi）中这一段受人称道的文字：——
我们的公爵夫人妹妹，在囹圄之中
 

 神态如何？
高贵得很：我来描绘她。
 

 她忧戚，如久惯于此的人；而且她似乎
 

 与其说在躲避苦难的尽头，
 

 不如说在迎它前来：这等的举止如此高贵，
 

 竟赋予逆境以一种庄严3
 

 你可以在她的泪水中，比在她的微笑中，
 

 更清晰地辨认出可爱的形貌；
 

 她会一连沉思好几个钟头；4 而她的沉默，
 

 在我看来，比她开口言说表达得更多。

===Original===
Now set against this the well-known passage from *Twelfth Night* where the Duke asks and Viola answers a question about someone unknown to him and invented by her﻿—a mere phantasm, in short: yet note how much more definite is the language:﻿—

===Chinese===
再把《第十二夜》（Twelfth Night）里那段著名的文字拿来同它对照——公爵在那里发问，薇奥拉（Viola）作答，所答的是一位他不认识、由她凭空虚构的人物——总之是一个纯粹的幻影：但请留意，这里的语言何等更为确切：——

===Original===
My father had a daughter lov’d a man;
 

 As it might be, perhaps, were I a woman,
 

 *I* should your lordship.
A blank, my lord. She never told her love,
 

 But let concealment, like a worm i’ the bud,
 

 Feed on her damask cheek; she pined in thought,
 

 And with a green and yellow melancholy
 

 She sat like Patience on a monument
 

 Smiling at grief. Was not this love indeed?

===Chinese===
我的父亲有一个女儿，她爱过一个男子；
 

 假如——也许——我是个女人，
 

 *我*也会爱上殿下这样的人。
一片空白，殿下。她从不向人吐露她的爱情，
 

 只让「隐藏」（concealment），像蓓蕾中的一条蛀虫，
 

 啃食她绯红的面颊；她为相思而憔悴，
 

 带着一种青黄交加的忧郁
 

 她坐着，像一座纪念碑上的「忍耐」（Patience）
 

 向着悲哀微笑。这难道不是爱情么？

===Original===
Observe (apart from the dramatic skill of it) how, when Shakespeare *has* to use the abstract noun “concealment,” on an instant it turns into a visible worm “feeding” on the visible rose; how, having to use a second abstract word “patience,” at once he solidifies it in tangible stone.

===Chinese===
请留意（先撇开其中的戏剧技巧不谈）：莎士比亚*不得不*用抽象名词「隐藏」（concealment）时，它一瞬间就变成一条看得见的蛀虫，在看得见的玫瑰花上「啃食」；他不得不再用一个抽象词「忍耐」（patience）时，立刻就把它凝固成摸得到的石头。

===Original===
Turning to prose, you may easily assure yourselves that men who have written learnedly on the art agree in treating our maxim﻿—to prefer the concrete term to the abstract, the particular to the general, the definite to the vague﻿—as a canon of rhetoric. Whately has much to say on it. The late Mr. E. J. Payne, in one of his admirable prefaces to Burke (prefaces too little known and valued, as too often happens to scholarship hidden away in a schoolbook), illustrated the maxim by setting a passage from Burke’s speech “On Conciliation with America” alongside a passage of like purport from Lord Brougham’s *Inquiry Into the Policy of the European Powers*. Here is the deadly parallel:﻿—
Burke

===Chinese===
转到散文上来，你们可以轻易地令自己确信：凡曾就这门艺术写过专门论述的人，都一致把我们的箴言——宁取具体词而不取抽象词，宁取特殊而不取一般，宁取确切而不取含混——奉为修辞学的一条准则。惠特利（Whately）对此有许多话可说。已故的佩恩先生（Mr. E. J. Payne）在他为伯克（Burke）所作的一篇令人叹服的序言里（这些序言知者太少、重之太轻——埋没在教科书里的学问往往如此遭际），演示了这条箴言：他把伯克《论与美洲和解》（On Conciliation with America）演说中的一段，与布鲁厄姆勋爵（Lord Brougham）《欧洲列强政策探究》（Inquiry Into the Policy of the European Powers）中一段大意相仿的文字并排放置。致命的对照如下：——
伯克（Burke）

===Original===
> In large bodies the circulation of power must be less vigorous at the extremities. Nature has said it. The Turk cannot govern Ægypt and Arabia and Curdistan as he governs Thrace; nor has he the same dominion in Crimea and Algiers which he has at Brusa and Smyrna. Despotism itself is obliged to truck and huckster. The Sultan gets such obedience as he can. He governs with a loose rein, that he may govern at all; and the whole of the force and vigour of his authority in his centre is derived from a prudent relaxation in all his borders.

===Chinese===
> 在庞大的机体里，权力的循环到了四肢末端必然较为无力。自然已经这样说了。土耳其人治理色雷斯（Thrace）的那种治理法，用到埃及（Ægypt）、阿拉伯（Arabia）和库尔德斯坦（Curdistan）就行不通；他在克里米亚（Crimea）和阿尔及尔（Algiers）的统治权，也决比不上他在布尔萨（Brusa）和士麦那（Smyrna）的。连专制本身也不得不讨价还价、以货易货。苏丹能弄到多少服从就弄多少。他放松缰绳去统治，为的是还能统治得下去；而他在中心地带权威的全部力量与元气，都得自他对四境边陲一番审慎的放松。

===Original===
Brougham

===Chinese===
布鲁厄姆（Brougham）

===Original===
> In all the despotisms of the East, it has been observed that the further any part of the empire is removed from the capital, the more do its inhabitants enjoy some sort of rights and privileges: the more inefficacious is the power of the monarch; and the more feeble and easily decayed is the organisation of the government.

===Chinese===
> 在东方一切专制国家中，人们观察到：帝国任何一部分离首都愈远，其居民便愈能享有某种权利与特权：君主的权力便愈无效能；政府的组织便愈衰弱、愈易于败坏。

===Original===
You perceive that Brougham has transferred Burke’s thought to his own page: but will you not also perceive how pitiably, by dissolving Burke’s vivid particulars into smooth generalities, he has enervated its hold on the mind?

===Chinese===
你们看得出，布鲁厄姆把伯克的思想搬到了自己的书页上：但你们岂不也看得出，他把伯克那些鲜明的特殊细节溶成一团平滑的泛泛之论，又是何等可悲地松懈了它对心智的把握？

===Original===
“This particularising style,” comments Mr. Payne, “is the essence of Poetry; and in Prose it is impossible not to be struck with the energy it produces. Brougham’s passage is excellent in its way: but it pales before the flashing lights of Burke’s sentences.” The best instances of this energy of style, he adds, are to be found in the classical writers of the seventeenth century. “When South says, ‘An Aristotle was but the rubbish of an Adam, and Athens but the rudiments of Paradise,’ he communicates more effectually the notion of the difference between the intellect of fallen and of unfallen humanity than in all the philosophy of his sermons put together.”

===Chinese===
佩恩先生评道：「这种铺陈细节的文体，是诗之精髓；而在散文中，它所产生的能量，也不可能不令人震动。布鲁厄姆的段落就其自身而言是出色的：但在伯克句子迸射的光焰面前，它便黯然失色。」他补充说，这种文体力量的最佳范例，见于十七世纪的古典作家。「当索斯（South）说：『一个亚里士多德（Aristotle），不过是亚当（Adam）身上扫下来的一点垃圾；雅典，不过是乐园的初阶』时，他传达堕落人类与未堕人类之间智力差异的概念，比他全部布道词里的哲学加在一起还要有效。」

===Original===
You may agree with me, or you may not, that South in this passage is expounding trash; but you will agree with Mr. Payne and me that he uttered it vividly.

===Chinese===
索斯在这一段里宣讲的是不是糟粕，你们可以同意我，也可以不同意；但你们会同意我和佩恩先生的：他讲得鲜明生动。

===Original===
Let me quote to you, as a final example of this vivid style of writing, a passage from Dr. John Donne far beyond and above anything that ever lay within South’s compass:﻿—

===Chinese===
作为这种鲜明文体的最后一个例子，让我为诸位引一段约翰·多恩博士（Dr. John Donne）的文字，它远远超出、远远高过索斯才具所曾企及的任何东西：——

===Original===
> The ashes of an Oak in the Chimney are no epitaph of that Oak, to tell me how high or how large that was; it tells me not what flocks it sheltered while it stood, nor what men it hurt when it fell. The dust of great persons’ graves is speechless, too; it says nothing, it distinguishes nothing. As soon the dust of a wretch whom thou wouldest not, as of a prince whom thou couldest not look upon will trouble thine eyes if the wind blow it thither; and when a whirle-wind hath blown the dust of the Churchyard into the Church, and the man sweeps out the dust of the Church into the Churchyard, who will undertake to sift those dusts again and to pronounce, This is the Patrician, this is the noble flowre [flour], this the yeomanly, this the Plebeian bran? So is the death of Iesabel (Iesabel was a Queen) expressed. They shall not say “This is Iesabel”; not only not wonder that it is, nor pity that it should be; but they shall not say, they shall not know, “This is Iesabel.”

===Chinese===
> 一株橡树在烟囱里的灰烬，并不是那株橡树的墓志铭；它说不出它当年有多高、有多粗；它不曾告诉我，它矗立时荫蔽过哪些畜群，倒下时又压伤了哪些人。伟人坟茔的尘土，也同样无言；它什么也不说，什么也不分辨。风若把尘土往那里一吹，一个你不屑一顾的贱民的尘土，同一个你不敢逼视的君王的尘土，同样会迷住你的眼睛；而当一阵旋风把墓地的尘土刮进教堂、教堂的杂役又把教堂的尘土扫回墓地时，谁还肯承担把这些尘土重新筛开、并断言：这是贵族，这是高贵的细面（flowre〔flour〕），这是自耕农的，这是平民的麸皮？耶洗别（Iesabel）的死（耶洗别原是位王后），就是这样昭示的。他们必不说「这是耶洗别」；不仅不惊异其如此、不怜悯其当此；他们必不说、也必不知道，「这是耶洗别」。

===Original===
Carlyle noted of Goethe, “his emblematic intellect, his never-failing tendency to transform into *shape*, into *life*, the feeling that may dwell in him. Everything has form, has visual excellence: the poet’s imagination bodies forth the forms of things unseen, and his pen turns them into shape.”

===Chinese===
卡莱尔（Carlyle）论及歌德（Goethe）时指出：「他那象征性的心智，他那从不衰竭的倾向——把或许栖居于他心中的情感转化为*形*（shape）、转化为*生命*（life）。万物皆有形态，皆有可见的卓越：诗人的想象把未见之物的形体呼唤出来，他的笔再把它们铸成形状。」

===Original===
Perpend this, Gentlemen, and maybe you will not hereafter set it down to my reproach that I wasted an hour of a May morning in a denunciation of Jargon, and in exhorting you upon a technical matter at first sight so trivial as the choice between abstract and definite words.

===Chinese===
细想细想这个吧，诸位先生；这样，日后你们或许就不会把一桩过失记在我的账上，怪我白白浪费了五月早晨的一个钟头去声讨套话，去就一件乍看如此琐屑的技术问题——抽象词与确切词之间的抉择——向诸位谆谆劝勉。

===Original===
A lesson about writing your language may go deeper than language; for language (as in a former lecture I tried to preach to you) is your reason, your *λὀγος*. So long as you prefer abstract words, which express other men’s summarised concepts of things, to concrete ones which as near as can be reached to things themselves and are the firsthand material for your thoughts, you will remain, at the best, writers at secondhand. If your language be Jargon, your intellect, if not your whole character, will almost certainly correspond. Where your mind should go straight, it will dodge: the difficulties it should approach with a fair front and grip with a firm hand it will be seeking to evade or circumvent. For the Style is the Man, and where a man’s treasure is there his heart, and his brain, and his writing, will be also.

===Chinese===
关于写自己语言的一课，可以比语言更深；因为语言（正如我在前一次讲座中试图向诸位宣讲的）就是你们的理性，你们的 *λὀγος*（逻各斯）。只要你们还偏爱抽象词——它们表达的是旁人对事物的概括概念——而不爱具体词——它们尽可能接近事物本身，是你们思想的第一手材料——那么你们至多也只能做个二手的写作者。倘若你们的语言是套话，那么你们的智识，即便不说整个品性，也几乎必定与之相当。在心智本应径直前往的地方，它将躲闪：本应堂堂正正迎上前去、用坚定的手攥住的困难，它将设法回避或者绕开。因为风格即人；一个人的财宝在哪里，他的心、他的头脑、他的文章，也必在哪里。

## Lecture VI On the Capital Difficulty of Prose / VI 论散文之大难点

===Original===
Thursday, May 15

===Chinese===
5 月 15 日，星期四

===Original===
Today, Gentlemen, leaving the Vanity Fair of Jargon behind us, we have to essay a difficult country; of which, though fairly confident of his compass-bearings, your guide confesses, that wide tracts lie outside his knowledge—outside of anything that can properly be called his knowledge. I feel indeed somewhat as Gideon must have felt when he divided his host on the slopes of Mount Gilead, warning back all who were afraid. In asking the remnant to follow as attentively as they can, I promise only that, if Heaven carry us safely across, we shall have “broken the back” of the desert.

===Chinese===
诸位先生，今天，把套话（Jargon）的名利场（Vanity Fair）抛在身后，我们要尝试进入一片艰难的国度；关于这片国度，你们的向导虽自认对罗盘方位把握得还算可靠，却必须承认，其中有大片大片的疆域在他知识之外——在任何配称他知识的东西之外。我此刻的心情，实在有些像基甸（Gideon）当年在基列山（Mount Gilead）山坡上分选兵员时必有的心情，他把一切惧怕的人都劝了回去。既请余下的人尽其所能地凝神相随，我所能允诺的只是：若上苍保佑我们平安渡过，我们便算是「折断了这片荒漠的脊梁」——闯过了它最艰难的一段。

===Original===
In my last lecture but one, then—and before our small interlude with Jargon—the argument had carried us, more or less neatly, up to this point: that the capital difficulty of verse consisted in saying ordinary unemotional things, of bridging the flat intervals between high moments. This point, I believe, we made effectively enough.

===Chinese===
那么，在上上次的讲座里——也就是我们就套话作那次小小插叙之前——论证已经多少利落地把我们带到了这样一点：诗的大难点，在于说平常的、不动情感的事物，在于架桥跨越高昂时刻（high moments）之间的平淡间隔。这一点，我相信我们已阐发得足够有力。

===Original===
Now, for logical neatness, we should be able to oppose a corresponding point, that the capital difficulty of prose consists in saying extraordinary things, in running it up from its proper level to these high emotional, musical, moments. And mightily convenient that would be, Gentlemen, if I were here to help you to answer scientific questions about prose and verse instead of helping you, in what small degree I can, to write. But in Literature (which, let me remind you yet once again, is an art) you cannot classify as in a science.

===Chinese===
而如今，为求逻辑上的齐整，我们本应能摆出一个对偶的论点：散文的大难点在于说不平凡的事物，在于驱策它从自身固有的水位攀升到那些情绪高昂、乐音悠扬的时刻。诸位先生，倘若我站在这里是为了帮你们解答关于散文与诗的科学问题，而不是在我力所能及的些微之处帮助你们写作，那可就万分便利了。但在文学里（请容我再次提醒你们，文学是一门艺术），你们不能像在科学里那样分类。

===Original===
Pray attend while I impress on you this most necessary warning. In studying literature, and still more in studying to write it, distrust all classification! All classifying of literature intrudes “science” upon an art, and is artificially “scientific”; a trick of pedants, that they may make it the easier to examine you on things with which no man should have any earthly concern, as I am sure he will never have a heavenly one. Beetles, minerals, gases, may be classified; and to have them classified is not only convenient but a genuine advance of knowledge. But if you had to *make* a beetle, as men are making poetry, how much would classification help? To classify in a science is necessary for the purpose of that science: to classify when you come to art is at the best an expedient, useful to some critics and to a multitude of examiners. It serves the art-critic to talk about Tuscan, Flemish, Pre-Raphaelite, schools of painting. The expressions are handy, and we know more or less what they intend. Just so handily it may serve us to talk about “Renaissance poets,” “the Elizabethans,” “the Augustan age.” But such terms at best cannot be scientific, precise, determinate, as for examples the terms “inorganic,” “mammal,” “univalve,” “Old Red Sandstone” are scientific, precise, determinate. An animal is either a mammal or it is not: you cannot say as assuredly that a man is or is not an Elizabethan. We call Shakespeare an Elizabethan and the greatest of Elizabethans, though as a fact he wrote his most famous plays when Elizabeth was dead. Shirley was but seven years old when Elizabeth died; yet (if “Elizabethan” have any meaning but a chronological one) Shirley belongs to the Elizabethan firmament, albeit but as a pale star low on the horizon: whereas Donne—a post-Elizabethan if ever there was one—had by 1603 reached his thirtieth year and written almost every line of those wonderful lyrics which for a good sixty years gave the dominant note to Jacobean and Caroline poetry.

===Chinese===
请留神听我把这条最必要的警告印进你们心里。在研究文学时，尤其是在研习写作文学时，要提防一切分类！凡对文学的分类，都是把「科学」硬闯进一门艺术，都是人为地「科学化」；那是学究们的把戏，好让他们更容易就一些世人本不该有半点尘俗牵累的事物考问你们——正如我确信，他们休想对这些事物有半点天上的挂怀。甲虫、矿物、气体，尽可以分类；给它们分了类，不仅方便，而且是知识货真价实的进步。但倘若你们必须去*制造*一只甲虫，就像人们正在制造诗歌那样，分类又能帮上多少忙？在科学中分类，为的是那门科学自身的目的：而到了艺术跟前还要分类，充其量只是一种权宜之计，对某些批评家和一大群考官有用。艺术批评家谈托斯卡纳派、弗拉芒派、前拉斐尔派的绘画流派，原也无妨。这些说法很顺手，我们也大致知道它们指的是什么。同样顺手地，我们谈「文艺复兴诗人」「伊丽莎白时代派」「奥古斯都时代」，或许也无妨。但这类名目充其量做不到科学、精确、界说分明，不像「无机体」「哺乳动物」「单壳类」「老红砂岩」这些术语那样是科学的、精确的、界说分明的。一只动物要么是哺乳动物，要么不是：你们却不能同样有把握地说，一个人是或不是伊丽莎白派。我们把莎士比亚（Shakespeare）称作伊丽莎白派，而且是伊丽莎白派中最伟大的一位，尽管事实上，他那些最著名的剧作写成之时，伊丽莎白已经死了。伊丽莎白驾崩时，雪莉（Shirley）年仅七岁；然而（倘若「伊丽莎白派」除了纪年之外还有什么别的意义）雪莉仍属于伊丽莎白的那片星空，尽管只是低悬在地平线上的一颗暗星：而多恩（Donne）——若说有谁够格称后伊丽莎白派，那就数他——到 1603 年已届三十，那些精妙的抒情诗他几乎已行行写就，而在日后足足六十年间，正是这些抒情诗为詹姆士时代与查理时代的诗歌定下了主导的音调。

===Original===
In treating of an art we classify for handiness, not for purposes of exact knowledge; and man (*improbus homo*) with his wicked inventions is forever making fools of our formulae. Be consoled—and, if you are wise, thank Heaven—that genius uses our best-laid logic to explode it.

===Chinese===
谈论一门艺术时，我们分类是为了顺手，不是为了求精确的知识；而人——*improbus homo*（那顽劣不餍的人）——凭着他种种刁钻的发明，永远在愚弄我们的公式。且宽心吧——你们若明智，还当感谢上苍——天才正拿我们布置得最周密的逻辑去把它炸个粉碎。

===Original===
Be consoled, at any rate, on finding that after deciding the capital difficulty of prose to lie in saying extraordinary things, in running up to the high emotional moments, the prose-writers explode and blow our admirable conclusions to ruins.

===Chinese===
无论如何，且在下面这件事里宽心吧：我们既已判定，散文的大难点在于说不平凡的事物、在于一路攀升到情绪高昂的时刻，散文作家们却轰然爆炸，把我们那套可钦可佩的结论炸成了废墟。

===Original===
You see, we gave them the chance to astonish us when we defined prose as “a record of human thought, dispensing with metre and using rhythm laxly.” When you give genius leave to use something laxly, at its will, genius will pretty surely get the better of you.

===Chinese===
你们看，当我们把散文定义为「人类思想的记录，不用格律（metre），而对节奏（rhythm）的运用从宽」时，我们就已经亲手给了他们让我们惊异的机会。你们一旦容许天才把某样东西用得从宽、任凭己意，天才十有八九要占你们的上风。

===Original===
Observe, now, following the story of English prose, what has happened. Its difficulty—the inherent, the native disability of prose—is to handle the high emotional moments which more properly belong to verse. Well, we strike into the line of our prose-writers, say as early as Malory. We come on this; of the Passing of Arthur:—

===Chinese===
现在请看，循着英语散文的历程，都发生了些什么。散文的困难——散文与生俱来的、天然的缺陷——在于驾驭那些本该更恰当地归属诗的高昂时刻。好，让我们切入散文作家们的队列，早可早至马洛礼（Malory）。我们撞见了这样一段，讲亚瑟之死（the Passing of Arthur）：——

===Original===
> “My time hieth fast,” said the king. Therefore said Arthur unto Sir Bedivere, “Take thou Excalibur my good sword, and go with it to yonder water side; and when thou comest there I charge thee throw my sword in that water and come again and tell me what there thou seest.” “My lord,” said Bedivere, “your commandment shall be done; and lightly bring you word again.” So Sir Bedivere departed, and by the way he beheld that noble sword, that the pommel and the haft was all of precious stones, and then he said to himself, “If I throw this rich sword in the water, thereof shall never come good, but harm and loss.” And then Sir Bedivere hid Excaliber under a tree. And so, as soon as he might, he came again unto the king, and said he had been at the water and had thrown the sword into the water, “What saw thou there?” said the king, “Sir,” he said, “I saw nothing but waves and winds.”

===Chinese===
> 「我的时日飞逝，」国王说。于是亚瑟对贝迪维尔爵士（Sir Bedivere）说道：「把我那把好剑王者之剑（Excalibur）拿去，携它到那边水畔；你到了那里，我命你把我的剑掷入水中，然后回来告诉我，你在那里看见了什么。」「主上，」贝迪维尔说，「您的命令必当遵行；我很快便回来向您复命。」于是贝迪维尔爵士动身而去，一路端详着那柄名贵的宝剑——只见剑的柄头与剑柄通体皆是宝石——他随即自忖道：「我若把这柄贵重的剑掷入水里，非但永远生不出好处，反倒生出祸害与损失。」于是贝迪维尔爵士把王者之剑藏在一棵树下。随后他尽快回到国王那里，说自己已到过水边，已把剑掷入水中。「你在那里看见了什么？」国王问。「主上，」他说，「我什么也没看见，只有波浪和风。」

===Original===
Now I might say a dozen things of this and of the whole passage that follows, down to Arthur’s last words. Specially might I speak to you of the music of its monosyllables—“‘What sawest you there?’ said the king… ‘Do as well as thou mayest; for in me is no trust for to trust in. For I will into the Vale of Avilion, to heal me of my grievous wound. And if thou hear never more of me, pray for my soul.’” But, before making comment at all, I shall quote you another passage; this from Lord Berners’ translation of Froissart, of the death of Robert Bruce:—

===Chinese===
关于这一段，以及随后直到亚瑟临终遗言的整段文字，我本可以讲上十来层意思。我尤其可以向你们讲讲它单音节词的音乐——「『你在那里看见了什么？』国王说……『你尽力而为吧；因为我身上已无可倚恃之处可供倚恃。因为我要前往阿维隆之谷（Vale of Avilion），疗治我沉重的创伤。你若再也听不到我的消息，就请为我的灵魂祈祷。』」但在妄加评论之前，我还要给你们引一段文字；这段出自伯纳斯勋爵（Lord Berners）所译的傅华萨（Froissart），讲罗伯特·布鲁斯（Robert Bruce）之死：——

===Original===
> It fortuned that King Robert of Scotland was right sore aged and feeble: for he was greatly charged with the great sickness, so that there was no way for him but death. And when he felt that his end drew near, he sent for such barons and lords of his realm as he trusted best, and showed them how there was no remedy with him, but he must needs leave this transitory life.… Then he called to him the gentle knight, Sir William Douglas, and said before all the lords, “Sir William, my dear friend, ye know well that I have had much ado in my days to uphold and sustain the right of this realm; and when I had most ado I made a solemn vow, the which as yet I have not accomplished, whereof I am right sorry; the which was, if I might achieve and make an end of all my wars, so that I might once have brought this realm in rest and peace, then I promised in my mind to have gone and warred on Christ’s enemies, adversaries to our holy Christian faith. To this purpose mine heart hath ever intended, but our Lord would not consent thereto… And sith it is so that my body can not go, nor achieve that my heart desireth, I will send the heart instead of the body, to accomplish mine avow… I will, that as soon as I am trespassed out of this world, that ye take my heart out of my body, and embalm it, and take of my treasure as ye shall think sufficient for that enterprise, both for yourself and such company as ye will take with you, and present my heart to the Holy Sepulchre, whereas our Lord lay, seeing my body can not come there. And take with you such company and purveyance as shall be appertaining to your estate. And, wheresoever ye come, let it be known how ye carry with you the heart of King Robert of Scotland, at his instance and desire to be presented to the Holy Sepulchre.” Then all the lords, that heard these words, wept for pity.

===Chinese===
> 且说苏格兰国王罗伯特，年事已高，衰弱不堪：他为大症所困，沉疴压身，除死之外已无路可走。他自觉大限将近，便召来国中他最信得过的那些男爵与贵族，告知他们：自己已无药可救，必须撇下这易逝的生命了。……随后他把那位温厚的骑士威廉·道格拉斯爵士（Sir William Douglas）唤到跟前，当着众贵族的面说道：「威廉爵士，我亲爱的朋友，你深知我平生费了多少周折，来维护并撑持这王国的正统；而在最艰难的时候，我曾立下一道庄重的誓愿，至今尚未了结，为此我深感憾恨；那誓愿是：倘若我能征伐到底、平息一切战事，终能使这王国得享安宁太平，我便在心中许诺，要亲身出征，讨伐基督的仇敌、我们神圣基督教信仰的敌对者。我的心愿一直在此，只是我主不肯应允……既然我这身体去不成，无法达成心之所愿，我便要遣心代身，以了此誓……我愿在我一旦离世之后，你便将我的心自遗体中取出，以香料防腐，并从我的财宝中取用你以为足以成事之数，为你自己也为你愿携同的随行人等；然后携我的心前往圣墓（Holy Sepulchre）——我主曾经安卧之处——呈献于彼，既然我的身体不能亲到。你也要携带与你身份相称的随从与行装。无论你行至何处，都要让人知晓：你随身携着苏格兰国王罗伯特的心，是奉他的恳请与心愿，前来呈献于圣墓。」众贵族听闻此言，无不恻然泣下。

===Original===
There, in the fifteenth century and early in the sixteenth, you have Malory and Berners writing beautiful English prose; prose the emotion of which (I dare to say) you must recognise if you have ears to hear. So you see that already our English prose not only achieves the “high moment,” but seems to obey it rather and be lifted by it, until we ask ourselves, “Who could help writing nobly, having to tell how King Arthur died or how the Bruce?” Yes, but I bid you observe that Malory and Berners are both relating what, however noble, is quite simple, quite straightforward. It is when prose attempts to *philosophise*, to *express thoughts* as well as to relate simple sayings and doings—it is then that the trouble begins. When Malory has to philosophise death, to *think* about it, this is as far as he attains:—

===Chinese===
看哪，在十五世纪与十六世纪初叶，你们已见到马洛礼与伯纳斯写出的美丽英语散文；这散文的情感（我敢说）你们只消有耳朵就必能听出。于是你们看到，那时我们的英语散文不仅已经达到「高昂时刻」，而且简直像是服从它、被它托举而起，直到我们不禁自问：「既要讲述亚瑟王怎样死去、布鲁斯王怎样死去，谁能写得不高贵？」是这么回事，不过我请你们注意：马洛礼与伯纳斯所讲述的东西，无论何等高贵，都极其简单、极其直截。而当散文试图*作哲思*、试图不仅记叙简单的言行、还要*表达思想*时——麻烦便由此开始。当马洛礼不得不对死亡作哲思、不得不*思考*死亡时，他所能达到的不过如此：——

===Original===
> “Ah, Sir Lancelot,” said he, “thou wert head of all Christian Knights! And now I dare say,” said Sir Ector, “that, Sir Lancelot, there thou liest, thou were never matched of none earthly hands; and thou were the curtiest knight that ever bare shield: and thou were the truest friend to thy lover that ever strood horse, and thou were the truest lover of a sinful man that ever loved woman; and thou were the kindest man that ever strooke with sword; and thou were the goodliest person that ever came among press of knights; and thou were the meekest man and gentlest that ever sat in hall among ladies; and thou were the sternest Knight to thy mortal foe that ever put spear in the rest.”

===Chinese===
> 「啊，兰斯洛特爵士（Sir Lancelot），」他说，「你曾是全体基督徒骑士之首！而今我敢说，」埃克托爵士（Sir Ector）说，「兰斯洛特爵士啊，你躺在那里了：论武艺，尘世之手从未有人与你匹敌；你是历来执盾骑士中最谦恭知礼的：你是历来跨鞍者当中待心上人最忠实的朋友，是历来有罪凡人爱恋女子者当中最忠贞的情人；你是历来挥剑者当中最仁厚的；你是历来置身骑士丛中最堂堂的；你是历来坐于厅堂仕女之间最温良最谦和的；你是历来置长矛于架上者当中对付死敌最凛然的骑士。」

===Original===
Beautiful again, I grant! But note you that, eloquent as he can be on the virtues of his dead friend, when Sir Ector comes to the thought of death itself all he can accomplish is, “And now I dare say that, Sir Lancelot, there thou liest.”

===Chinese===
我承认，这又是美的！但请你们注意：埃克托爵士纵然能就亡友的美德说得这般雄辩，可一碰到死亡本身这个念头，他能成就的，不过是「而今我敢说，兰斯洛特爵士啊，你躺在那里了」一句而已。

===Original===
Let us make a leap in time and contrast this with Tyndale and the translators of our Bible, how they are able to make St. Paul speak of death:—

===Chinese===
让我们在时间上向前一跃，拿这个对照一下廷代尔（Tyndale）与我们圣经的译者们，看他们能叫圣保罗（St. Paul）把死亡说得怎样：——

===Original===
> So when this corruptible shall have put on incorruption, and this mortal shall have put on immortality, then shall be brought to pass the saying that is written, Death is swallowed up in victory. O death, where is thy sting? O grave, where is thy victory?

===Chinese===
> 这必朽坏的既穿上不朽，这必死的既穿上不死，那时经上所记「死被得胜吞灭」的话就应验了。死啊，你的毒钩在哪里？坟墓啊，你的胜利在哪里？

===Original===
There you have something clean beyond what Malory or Berners could compass: there you have a different kind of high moment—a high moment of philosophising: there you have emotion impregnated with thought. It was necessary that our English verse even after Chaucer, our English prose after Malory and Berners, should overcome this most difficult gap (which stands for a real intellectual difference) if it aspired to be what today it is—a language of the first class, comparable with Greek and certainly no whit inferior to Latin or French.

===Chinese===
这里你们所见的，已全然超出马洛礼或伯纳斯所能达到的范围：这是一种不同种类的高昂时刻——一种哲思的高昂时刻：这里是浸透了思想的情感。我们的英语诗即便在乔叟（Chaucer）之后、我们的英语散文在马洛礼与伯纳斯之后，都必须跨越这道最艰难的鸿沟（它代表着一道实实在在的智性差异），才能有望成为它今日的模样——一等的语言，可与希腊语比肩，而且绝不比拉丁语或法语逊色半分。

---

===Original===
Let us leave prose for a moment, and see how Verse threw its bridge over the gap. If you would hear the note of Chaucer at its deepest, you will find it in the famous exquisite lines of the Prioress’ Prologue:—

===Chinese===
让我们暂且撇开散文，看看诗是怎样把桥架过这道鸿沟的。你们若要听乔叟最深沉的音调，会在《女修道院院长的序幕》（Prioress' Prologue）那著名而精美的诗行里找到：——

===Original===
> O moder mayde! O maydë moder fre!

> O bush unbrent, brenning in Moyses’ sight!

===Chinese===
> 啊，母亲而贞女！啊，贞女而自由的母亲！

> 啊，未尝焚毁的荆棘，在摩西眼前燃烧！

===Original===
in the complaint of Troilus, in the rapture of Griselda restored to her children:—

===Chinese===
